# MyNclex Sessions Log

Index of MyNclex work sessions. Detail lives in period files (monthly by
default) under `sessions/`; this file is the index. When logging a new
session, decide whether to append to the current period file or start a
new one. Newest first. Product-local — per the extraction rule in
CLAUDE.md.

---

## Session files (newest first)

- [2026-08 — **2026-08-23/24 — self-paced students stop being invisible, then the system starts chasing them:** branch `claude/work-session-f7fde6`, **6 commits, ONE migration** · ⭐ **SLICE B (`5cd5912`, migration `20260922120000`) — `progress.inactivity_nudge`, THE FIRST EMAIL NOBODY TRIGGERED**: every other key fires because a person DID something; this fires because they did **nothing**, so it has no anchor and needs a nightly pass to notice at all. ⚠⚠ **Which changes what keeps it honest** — an installment reminder compares against a due DATE so its window and fingerprint are belt-and-braces, but *"silent for 14 days" becomes true and STAYS true*, so the unique index works **alone**, and the subject is a PERSON, exactly the case where the stage must name the occurrence or the second email hits the index and **silently never sends**. ⭐ **Nudge 2 is 30 days after nudge 1 was SENT, not 44 days of silence** — Sam approved "44" and building it showed they are not equivalent: a student already 50 days quiet at launch would match BOTH absolute thresholds on the first run and get two emails the same morning. ⭐ **ONE DEFINITION OF "LAST SEEN", IN SQL**, called by the sweep AND the Progress page — written twice they drift, and the drift means the screen calling somebody "Stalled" that the system never wrote to. **Sam's calls in the build:** one admin switch not a panel of dials · programme-side object names (they share a flat namespace with bank objects) · the tutor sees that we wrote, via a DEFINER function returning a timestamp and never payload content. ⚠ Tested with the drain switched OFF because one candidate was a REAL third party, not a plus-addressed inbox; 3 of 11 selected, second run 0, off switch 0, everything restored. ⚠ **The rendered email is UNVERIFIED** — the preview needs COMMS_MANAGE and the test session is tutor-only; a template that throws is marked DEAD rather than sent, and nothing else catches it. **⚠⚠ AND A CORRECTION FELL OUT:** `transactional-email.md` marked `enrolment.access_expiring`/`access_expired` "blocked on access windows" — they were **never blocked**, the column shipped 2026-05-27 and the sweep has expired on it since 2026-06-08, so **a student's access has been ending in silence ever since**. · **SLICE A, earlier:** · A tutor running a **self-paced** programme could see who had paid and **nothing else** — no way to tell whether anybody had ever opened the curriculum, six months after cohort students got a progress dashboard. ⓘ **The code was already apologising for it in a comment** (*"self-paced avg completion is intentionally absent: it needs a programme-level aggregator that isn't built yet"*) and `progress-engine.md` listed it as deferred follow-on #1 — it sat there because nobody read the two together. ⭐⭐ **THE SESSION'S SHAPE WAS SET BY SAM REFUSING MY FRAMING TWICE.** I opened with a survey of the whole programme side and recommended a per-student "My Students" 360; he redirected to the actual hole (*"the per student list on cohort overview gives a picture of what the student is doing but this is not available for self paced students"*), which was the follow-on the docs had already identified and I had ranked fourth. Then, asked what a tutor wants to know, I produced a four-flavour triage list — and widening the frame to *what is a self-paced programme FOR* inverted it: comparable platforms surface **aggregate** for the content decision and **automation** for the individual nudge, and a per-student to-do list actively harms a product whose economics depend on staying low-touch. ⭐ Sam then rejected the split I built on top of that (*"if we ported the same idea… it captures the things you are saying and does not mean tutor should go check every day"*) and he was right on both counts: **a dashboard is passive, and the cohort view ALREADY contains the aggregate** — its per-activity rollup IS the drop-off curve, so I had offered as a separate build something that came free. **THE MODEL IS HIS**: *a self-paced programme is one cohort with late joins* — own start date (the day they bought), own end date (their access window), no release gates, no live sessions — and three of the four differences from the cohort query make it **simpler**. It also yielded a better column than mine: **joined**, because a completion % has no referent without a shared calendar. **⚠ THE ONE THING THAT COULD NOT BE PORTED WAS THE STATUS PILL** — the cohort classifier buckets on completion % of RELEASED material, fair only because a cohort in week 2 has released weeks 1–2; self-paced unlocks everything on day one, so **a student who joined yesterday would have read "At risk" on arrival**, every new student flagged as failing the moment they turned up. Replaced with engagement over time (Not started · Active · Stalled · Finished) + **"Ending soon" as an ORTHOGONAL overlay, never a fifth state** — somebody can be working hard and still about to lose the material. ⭐ **NO MIGRATION, AND NOT BY LUCK**: the `*_tutor_read` policies resolve ownership by walking activity → unit → programme → tutor and **never touch cohort**, so a cohortless student's data was always readable — nothing had ever asked. ⭐ **THE RENAME FIXED AN AMBIGUITY THAT ALREADY EXISTED** (Sam: *"if we going to call it progress then at the cohort, the overview pane should be called progress"*) — analytics were folded into the cohort's Overview tab in June, which made "Overview" the wrong word while the *programme* Overview stayed a grid of summary cards; the new surface only made it unignorable, since the same content would otherwise carry two names by delivery mode. ⓘ I had briefly argued AGAINST a separate page by over-reading that same June fold — it merged two **cohort tabs** and said nothing about programme level — and Sam corrected the misreading. **⚠⚠ AND AN ASSUMPTION THE CANONICAL DOC TAUGHT ME**: `main.md` said a self-paced access window is per-enrolment *"rather than bounded by cohort start/end dates"*, implying a cohort's IS bounded by its dates. It is not — access is frozen from the JOIN DATE in **both** modes, so one cohort's members hold different end dates. Dev has a cohort that ran 1–28 Jul 2026, is badged **Ended**, and whose students keep access until **24 Jun 2027**; the nightly sweep expires on `access_expires_at`, so that column, not the cohort's dates, is what actually cuts a student off — and it appeared on **no tutor surface at all**. Sam spotted the column was worth stealing before the reason was known; the data then made it a defect fix rather than a nicety. ⓘ *A wrong assumption in a canonical doc is worse than none: I read it, believed it, and wrote it into a code comment as fact.* ⓘ **"Last active" also stopped meaning "last thing they ticked done"** — attempts carry `last_activity_at`, so a student grinding through a quiz they never submit is visible instead of reading as dead; deliberately **not** `last_login_utc`, which is product-wide and answers "have they vanished entirely", not "have they abandoned THIS programme". ⚠ Dev data exercises only four of the six states — nobody has worked in 14 days, finished, or has a window closing inside 30 — so **Active / Finished / Ending soon are unverified on screen**. ⏭ **Open:** `progress.inactivity_nudge` (⭐ build it BEFORE expanding this surface — without it every stalled student becomes the tutor's problem by default, turning a low-touch product high-touch at a low-touch price) · the cross-cohort roll-up, deliberately unbundled · the per-student 360 · and `/tutor/students`, still a **counted KPI card pointing at an empty room**.](sessions/2026-08.md)
- [2026-08 — **2026-08-22 (later) — slice 3, then the public site nobody had looked at:** branch `claude/modest-nash-115a4a`, **6 commits, NO migration, Sam-tested, ⚠ ON BRANCH — not merged to `main`** · ⚠⚠ **THE PUBLIC NAV HAD NO MOBILE NAVIGATION AT ALL** — `.pub-nav .links` was `display:none` below the breakpoint with **nothing replacing it**, so on a phone the only public links were the brand, Log in and Help in the footer, and **`/readiness` and `/for-tutors` had NO entry point anywhere in the product** (one a product we sell, the other the tutor door built that same morning), on a **phone-first** audience; `/bank-access` and `/programmes` survived only because the landing page happens to link them in its body. ⭐ **Six mobile sweeps had run and every one covered surfaces you reach AFTER signing in** — nobody had checked what a stranger sees first. ⭐⭐ **Sam corrected me twice and was right twice**: I proposed wrapping the row to a second line (*"the standard known way is a hamburger"* — and the arguments I had under-weighted were that the app already teaches ≤768px-means-drawer, and that four links is today's count), then I called it "a new component" (*"why a new component? it's the existing top bar behaving differently on mobile"* — and his way carries the better argument he did not give: **two components would be two link lists that drift**). ⭐ His third question — *"is the public nav not different from the in-app nav?"* — killed my lean toward reusing the app's `m-*` drawer: checking rather than asserting showed reuse would have **worked** (tokens resolve, the risky rules are scoped to `.shell-root`), which is exactly what made it the tempting wrong answer, since the public site is a ported design system with its own vocabulary and the half most likely to be re-cut. ⭐ ***Share the hard-won behaviour; do not share the stylesheet*** — the drawer's Escape / scroll-lock / focus-move / close-when-the-viewport-grows logic was copied from the app's, the CSS was not. Also: hamburger **right** for thumb reach (diverging from the app's left drawer on purpose) · Log in **into** the drawer (Sam; I argued the discovery cost and was overruled) · the **inert GHS/USD toggle deleted**, it duplicated the working `.bkc-fx` and was the one element that kept its space on mobile while every real link was hidden · breakpoint **760 → 768**. **THE RENAME WAS AN UNFINISHED DECISION, NOT A NEW ONE** — 16 user-facing "QAcademy" strings became "Quademia", but the rule had been settled **2026-08-19** and written into one email template (*"QAcademy is the old name … it must not reach a reader"*) and exactly one file obeyed it: ⭐ **a decision recorded in one call site is not a decision that propagated**; comments keep the old name on purpose, and ⚠ the copyright line was **asked about rather than assumed** because a copyright notice names a legal entity (Sam: QAcademy is not registered). **THE MARK** — ⭐ cutting one out of AI art is not a crop: scanning for "first pixel unlike the background" catches the **drop shadow**, putting the centre 13px out and leaving a slate crescent on the rim that is invisible until rendered; scanning for the **cream ring** gives the true edge and says the mark is a **997×1051 ellipse**, not a circle. ⚠⚠ **AND THE BRANDING CHANGE THAT WOULD HAVE SHIPPED BROKEN: `metadataBase`** — `og:image` needs an absolute URL and Next falls back to `http://localhost:3000` unless it or `VERCEL_URL` is set, which on Cloudflare it never is, so production HTML would have pointed every crawler at localhost; ⭐⭐ **dev could not answer the question**, printing localhost with or without the fix because Next overrides it there for local previews, so it was settled by running a **production build** and reading the prerendered HTML. ⓘ Found on the way: `NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL` is set in neither wrangler.jsonc, `.env.local` nor either workflow, so every `?? 'https://nclex.quademia.com'` has always used its fallback. ⭐ *"Should the nav's M become the new icon?"* — answered **no**, and it settled something larger: **Quademia is the brand, MyNclex is a product name under it**, so MyNclex gets no mark of its own. ⏭ `/privacy` and `/terms` live in the `quademia-parent-site` repo and were not swept. **Slice 3 itself, earlier the same session:** (check `git log origin/prod` before believing that — this session opened by correcting the same flag on slice 2, which had been on prod as `de88294` for a day). **THE TUTOR-ONBOARDING ARC IS CLOSED** — all four doorways in §5 exist, and there is no longer any way of becoming a tutor that needs somebody to write SQL. ⭐ **Four things were called "decisions" and only two were** — the `/welcome` risk and "no migration needed" were *findings*, which change how carefully you test, not what you build; saying so up front turned a four-question list into two. ⚠⚠ **THE EMAIL WAS THE REAL GAP AND THE PLAN DID NOT SEE IT.** §10 said the invite "sends the setup link"; `TutorAddedByAdminPayload` had **no field for one**, and both its buttons pointed at `/tutor/profile` and `/tutor` — **behind a login the invitee cannot pass**, because the account has no password. ⓘ The template even carried a preview labelled *"Invited by email (slice 3)"*: the reuse was anticipated, the link was not. ⭐ **Fixed with a DIAL, not a second key** — `entry: LOG_IN | SET_UP`, mirroring `enrolment-added`, which settled the identical fork on 2026-08-12 as *"TWO DIALS, NOT FOUR EMAILS"*; §10's own test for splitting a key is *"shared facts, nothing else in common"* and here the facts **and the intent** are identical, so the planned `tutor.invited` was **retired without ever being built** (29 catalog entries, not 30). ⚠⚠ **ABSENCE OF THE DIAL MEANS `LOG_IN`, AND THAT IS A COMPATIBILITY RULE, NOT A DEFAULT** — `renderOutboxRow` renders from the **frozen payload alone**, so every row queued before this slice, **prod included**, arrives with no `entry` and must keep rendering the email it actually sent: *adding a field to a payload is adding it to history you have already sent.* ⚠ And the template **degrades rather than trusts** — I was about to write a discriminated union until I checked and found `payload` is `Record<string, unknown>` at the enqueue boundary, so nothing type-checks "SET_UP carries a link" **where it would matter**; with no link it prints the sign-in-code route instead of a dead button, which is a real way in because the account exists. ⭐⭐ **A PRE-EXISTING BUG FOUND ONLY BECAUSE SAM ASKED FOR A CHIP: `/welcome` NEVER STAMPED `last_login_utc`.** Written by `/login`'s two paths and nowhere else, so **anybody who arrived through an invite and stayed away read as "never signed in" forever** — wrong for every tutor-invited student and every pay-first buyer since those flows were built, not just for tutors. Finishing setup *is* a sign-in. ⓘ *The signal a new feature needs is a good way to discover the one the old features never wrote.* ⚠ The directory's **"Invited — not set up"** needs BOTH halves (`source = ADMIN_INVITE` **and** never signed in) — the second alone flags a *promoted* tutor who has not logged in lately, a different claim about different people — and it sits **under** the APPROVED pill rather than replacing it, because both are true. ⭐ **It replaced an instruction IN PLACE**: 1c's new-user branch already ended in *"ask them to register, then add them here"*, so there was never a moment with two buttons doing one job — and **1c had left the stylesheet ready too** (`.adt-names` with its ≤768px collapse, plus `.adt-receipt`), the third time in this arc a later slice found its groundwork already laid. ⚠ **The `/welcome` risk was SMALLER than the plan feared** — it called slice 3 "the one most able to break something that currently works", written before anyone checked; that mechanism has been live for tutor-added students and pay-first buyers since 2026-08-12 and `/router` already sent a TUTOR-only user to `/tutor`. What *did* need fixing was the copy: *"The email your tutor invited"* is true of one of the three flows that land there, and an admin-invited tutor has no tutor. ⚠⚠ **AN HOUR WENT TO A 404 THAT WAS NOT IN THE CODE** — `/admin/emails/preview` returned 404 from a file sitting on disk, and so did `/admin/bank/all`, `/cases` and `/trends`: **a whole class of nested routes was missing from this worktree's Turbopack route map**, and the generated `routes.d.ts` was partial enough to prove nothing either way. Clearing `.next` and restarting fixed it. ⓘ **And stopping the dev server did not stop it** — `TaskStop` killed the `npm run dev` wrapper and left its `next dev` child holding port 3000, so the restart moved silently to 3001 while the orphan kept serving the old build: *a "restarted" server on a different port is indistinguishable from a fix that did not work.* ⏭ **Deferred with a reason:** an invited tutor's name is typed by an admin who may have misheard it, correctable **exactly once** at `/welcome` and frozen after that, since nothing in the product edits identity — settled as **account-settings work** (§14) rather than a patch to the tutor directory, because the hole belongs to every audience. ⏭ **ARC LEFTOVERS, none blocking:** the student-facing suspension notice · the `private, no-store` gap repo-wide · the public nav hiding every link below 760px, which matters more now that one of those links is a door we want strangers to walk through.](sessions/2026-08.md)

- [2026-08 — **2026-08-22 — slice 2: the door opens, and twice it opened the wrong way:** branch `claude/tutor-onboarding-resume-e4fcbb`, **13 commits, ONE migration, Sam-tested throughout, ✅ MERGED AND RELEASED TO `prod` as `de88294`** (⚠ this entry said "ON BRANCH — not merged to `main`" for a full day after it shipped — the **fifth** stale status flag in four days, corrected 2026-08-22 by the next session, which is the only thing that has ever caught one). **Slice 2 complete: 2b · 2a-i · 2c · 2a-ii.** ⭐ **Re-cut with Sam before a line was written** — §5's rule branched on *whether you are signed in* and had no answer for the commonest real applicant, **logged out but already has an account**: an existing student on her phone, walked into a `signUp` that fails *after* she filled a long form. One door, routed by the **email**. ⭐ **The register-as-tutor toggle was dropped**, and the better reason is that `/register` is on a path to retirement (students increasingly arrive through checkout), so the toggle builds the tutor door onto a surface we expect to remove — which also deleted the slice's largest risk, since **2d existed as its own sub-slice only because it was the thing touching `/register`**. ⭐ **The form and the status page are ONE route** (it must read your record before rendering anything — that is how the SUSPENDED refusal works — so the form is just the state where no row is found), and **resubmission stops being a screen**. ⚠ **A suspended tutor can never re-apply**, which §9 was silent on and which would otherwise launder a suspension back into the queue. ⭐ Killing the toggle **moved a dependency rather than removing it** — 2c had to precede 2d; it now constrains 2a-ii. ⭐⭐ **ONE MIGRATION FOR FOUR SUB-SLICES**, because 1c and 1d were factored properly: the plan's own test — *"approve is 1c's action with a different trigger; if it needs new code, 1c was built too narrowly"* — **passed**, 1d's decision RPC already took APPROVED/REJECTED with every guard, 1b had shipped the queue's entire stylesheet unused, and `nclex_roles_self_insert_student` was already shaped for 2c's conversion (proven against the live policy: granting yourself TUTOR **refused**, granting STUDENT to somebody else **refused**, granting yourself STUDENT **succeeds** — the database enforces the action's shape rather than trusting it). ⭐⭐ **Sam asked whether the two admin drawers should merge; I argued no and was wrong** — the deciding argument is this arc's founding decision, **`nclex_tutors` is ONE ROW PER PERSON**, chosen over a per-request table precisely so one thing would represent the tutor; two partial views leave an admin with two screens for one record and no way to tell which is authoritative, and a rejected applicant appears in **both** already. Merged, **68 lines smaller** despite gaining sections, and *more* honest than either — ⚠ which forced both loaders to fetch every column, because a self-suppressing section cannot tell "they wrote no note" from "this page did not ask for the note". ⚠⚠ **A SECOND SUSPENSION EMAILED NOBODY, AND IT IS ON PROD** — the outbox de-dupes on `(event_key, subject_ref, stage)` and reads a unique violation as **success** (what makes Paystack's retries harmless); `stage` defaulted to `'-'`, *"a one-off"*. ⭐ **Right when a subject can experience an event once, wrong whenever `subject_ref` is a PERSON.** Refused insert, refusal read as success, action reporting it had emailed somebody it had not. ⚠ **The worst case was not suspension** — §9 exists so a rejected applicant can resubmit, so a *second* rejection is designed for and that email is the only thing carrying the new reason; built that morning, broken on arrival. ⭐⭐ **TWO THINGS SAM SENT BACK, BOTH RIGHT.** ① *"The whole thing is somehow over-engineered"* — we had settled **email-first** and I built a six-field form that found the collision at submit, then **changed an agreed design during implementation and reported it as a detail**, which is the worse half. ⚠ The security objection I changed it for **had an answer I never looked for** (Turnstile on the email step) — which then turned out to have a real one underneath: `lib/auth/turnstile.ts` says in capitals that a token validates **exactly once** and **Supabase** must spend it, so our own check can only confirm a token *arrived*; a script sends any string, and the thresholds are keyed by **email**, which is what an enumerator varies. So the email is asked first for the UX and Supabase answers it one step later for real. ⭐ **The rework DELETED the worst code in the slice** — no application is written before we know who they are, so the sessionStorage draft hand-off, an effect, a hydration argument and an `eslint-disable` with a paragraph defending it all vanished: *when the thing you argued hardest for disappears under the alternative, the alternative was right.* ② *"They don't see they are logged in, or how to log out"* — ⭐ for a role-less applicant **that route is the whole product**; they read "your application is with us" under a nav button inviting them to Log in. ⚠ Fixed on that route only, **measured not assumed**: every public page answers `no-cache, must-revalidate` with **no `private`**, so a name in the shared nav would put per-user content on every marketing page behind a header that does not say so. **Two more defects, invisible to every tool:** ⭐⭐ the apply page showed an **approved tutor a blank application form**, because the read leaned on RLS to scope itself and the policy is `user_id = auth.uid() OR has_permission(...)` — for an **admin** the OR matches every row, `maybeSingle()` returns null, and it would never have failed for an ordinary user (**an RLS policy with an OR in it is not a WHERE clause**); and **"Invalid Date"** in a trail from my own fixtures writing `+00` where the RPC writes `+00:00` — *a fixture in a shape the product cannot produce tests nothing and lies twice.* **Verified:** every RPC guard in rolled-back transactions · the `stage` fix across 18 decisions on 5 records, **decisions == distinct stages every time** · the collision path driven in the browser against a known address, creating **no user and no tutor row**. ⭐ **Sam's own testing is legible in the data**, which confirmed the three things I could not test myself: a role-less account rejected then holding STUDENT again (the conversion fired), a new account with `roles: []` and `source: REGISTRATION` (2a-ii end to end, derived source correct), and a resubmission at `stage: s2`. **All five emails SENT**, including the first this product has ever sent to itself. ⏭ **OPEN:** slice 3 (invite by email, the only path touching `/welcome`) · 1d's untouched *the student is never told* · ⚠⚠ **every page in this app answers `no-cache, must-revalidate`, authenticated ones included**, while CLAUDE.md rule #4 requires `private, no-store` — repo-wide, pre-existing, wants checking against the deployed Worker first · the public nav hiding every link below 760px, so on a phone the tutor doorway is URL-only · and whether `/admin/applications` should shrink to a pure inbox, since a to-do list that keeps completed items forever has stopped being one.](sessions/2026-08.md)

- [2026-08 — **2026-08-21 (evening) — slice 1d, and the switches that were not switches:** branch `claude/local-server-session-setup-cb52ba`, **11 commits, five migrations, dev-applied, Sam-tested, MERGED TO `main`** (not released — prod is a separate decision) (⚠ check `git log origin/main` before believing that — this line has been wrong twice in two days). Opened by correcting the previous entry, which claimed slice 1a–1c was unmerged with "four migrations, none on prod": all of it had merged AND released as `ce10dfa` the same evening, and the count had **never** been right — three, not four. ⭐⭐ **Sam's schema change:** reviewing the drawer he saw that the "full story" it promises could not exist, because `decision_reason` is scalar and keeps only the last decision. **`decision_history`, an append-only JSONB array**, and the rule banked with it — *a JSONB array is right while a history is short, bounded and read whole; a table is earned the day something queries across rows* — which keeps §13's rejection of an events table intact instead of smuggling it back a slice later. ⚠ The scalars stay authoritative (the catalogue filter and the status index need it indexable); **ONE SQL statement writes both**, and the entry's `from` reads the row's own status on the right-hand side of the UPDATE, so it is correct by construction and safe against two admins clicking at once. ⚠⚠ **THE NEW RPC RE-OPENED THE HOLE 1a SPENT A MIGRATION CLOSING** — `SECURITY DEFINER` runs as its definer, so the column privileges that stop a tutor writing `status` do nothing about a function that writes it for them, and any tutor also holding `TUTORS_MANAGE` could have lifted their own suspension. ⭐ **A column grant protects the table, not the functions over it.** ⚠⚠ **Two of §7's four "switches" were not switches:** the doc said "the same two views" and it is **four views and two RPCs** — and **checkout reads NONE of them**, resolving from base tables through the service role, so filtering every view in the database would not have stopped one sale; **and "money in flight stops" was not the reminder emails** — step 2c of the nightly sweep PAUSES students for arrears, so stopping the reminders alone would have meant quietly ceasing to ask for the money and then locking the student out for not paying it. ⭐ **The dialog is a promise:** four badges, four claims about what the code does, two of them false when the UI shipped. **Sam's two additions, both against the design:** `tutor.reinstated` (the catalogue had an email for taking a standing away and none for giving it back — *an account that writes when it removes something and goes silent when it restores it reads as punitive*), and a **confirm on reinstate**, whose real justification is that ⭐ since the trail became append-only **a misclick cannot be undone in the record** — state is recoverable, history is not. **Verified:** 7/7 backfilled rows identical to the old derivation · all four RPC guards proven in rolled-back transactions · suspending the tutor who owns all 11 published dev programmes takes the views **11/103/8/20 → 0/0/0/0 while 38 students stay ENROLLED and 115 curriculum rows are untouched** — that assertion *is* §7 · the enquiry RPC refuses, and still succeeds for a tutor in good standing · lint clean repo-wide. ⓘ **Everything on screen was verified by Sam and by nobody else** — he reported the outcome rather than a checklist, so the individual assertions are deliberately not itemised. ⭐⭐ **THEN HE ASKED THE TWO QUESTIONS THAT FOUND THE LAST HOLE.** *"Should we do something to their programmes?"* — argued down on the arc's own principle for the third time (unpublishing destroys which-were-already-drafts, and writes OUR decision into THEIR editorial column), **but his instinct about the COST was right**: two switches means every public surface must remember both, and I had already proved the risk by forgetting one; recorded as a follow-up to make "is this programme publicly live?" answerable in ONE place instead of re-derived in seven. And *"what does the student see?"* — which found a **live defect**: `nclex_enqueue_session_reminders` joined `nclex_users` for the tutor's NAME and never asked their standing, so the 07:00 cron kept emailing a suspended tutor's students *"your live class is tomorrow"*, §7's forbidden lie on a schedule. **Fixed** (`d734066`), in TWO places — ⚠ the tutor's own "send reminder now" button gated on **ownership**, which a suspended tutor still passes, since suspension revokes the ROLE and does not reassign their programmes. ⭐ Missed in 1d-v because **§7's table of consequences has four rows and this is a fifth** — *a table of consequences is a summary, not an inventory.* ⏭ **STILL OPEN:** nothing in the student interface says the tutor is suspended, and ⚠ **the reminder fix cannot close it** — reminders go out 7 days ahead and each class is announced exactly once, so a student may already hold a "see you Tuesday" for a class that is not happening. The fix stops the leak; only a notice on the student's screen mops up.](sessions/2026-08.md)

- [2026-08 — **2026-08-21 (later) — four sub-slices, and three defects no tool could see:** branch `claude/server-setup-12ef87`, **9 commits, three migrations, all merged to `main` and RELEASED TO `prod` the same evening** (⚠ this line first said "4 unmerged … none on prod" — see the correction at its foot) — the build half of the day the previous entry ends by naming. ⭐ **The Claude Design handoff was the best this repo has had** — it had read the plan doc and encoded its invariants, not just drawn screens (it knew `source` must not compete with `status` because nothing branches on it, and that the trail must be DERIVED because v1 has no events table). Every structural claim checked and held: all 12 `.ao-` classes exist, its bundled `enquiries.css` is **byte-identical**, every status tint already ships. ⚠ Its reference stylesheets are a strict **subset** of ours — copying them back would have silently reverted the `--surface` token and the mobile-sweep rules. Six changes on the way in, each a convention it could not see from outside. ⚠⚠ **THREE DEFECTS, NONE VISIBLE TO tsc, LINT OR TESTS:** ① ⭐ **self-approval** — `public_profile` moved onto a table that also holds `status`, carrying the old home's **whole-row** self-update policy with it, so any tutor could have run `update({ status: 'APPROVED' })` on their own row. **RLS cannot say "these columns but not those"**, so it took Postgres **column privileges** — the first in this repo — and it is proven to bite twice, synthetically and through Sam's own real save. Found only because 1a shipped alone. ② **`found` shadowed plpgsql's `FOUND`**, so the email-check RPC threw on *every* call — shipped typechecked, linted and **unexercised**. ③ **two-line cells rendered side by side** because they were `<span>`s; Sam saw it, and the fix was to reuse the enquiries board's own `.ao-cell-lead` rather than keep a second copy. ⭐⭐ **LEGACY was retired the day it was born, and the premise was mine:** Sam asked why the code should carry a branch for six rows that can never recur, and checking found `nclex_user_roles.granted_at` had recorded when every one of them became a tutor — the UI had spent the afternoon rendering "Unknown · predates the record" **over data we held**. The better reason was the column: a NULL `approved_at` had come to mean both "not yet" and "unknowable", and now means only the first. **Built: 1a** (`nclex_tutors`, the profile lift, the guard) · **1b** (directory + drawer) · **1c-i** (two narrow SECURITY DEFINER lookups, `grantTutorRole()`, the chooser + search, the welcome email) · **1c-ii** (the new-user path, four verdicts, both escape hatches). ⚠ **One RLS wall shapes all of it** — `nclex_users` is SUPER_ADMIN-only, so the list, the search and the email check would each have returned nothing for a `TUTORS_MANAGE` admin: **working perfectly for Sam and breaking the first time he delegates.** ⭐ **Sam's calls:** existing tutors shown greyed not hidden · **emails send instantly with the queue as the safety net** (`enqueueAndSend` — send now when a human is standing there who could fix a failure, queue plainly when nobody is; 0.5s enqueue→SENT, and it still returns *queued*, never *delivered*) · **the welcome email names no admin**, because a staff name in an outward mail is a disclosure made by accident the first time TUTORS_MANAGE is delegated · the free-tier line cut, because tiers do not exist. ⚠ He also caught **an ordering contradiction I introduced** — the doc said build 1c after 1a, four paragraphs below lettering that says 1b first; they are the same page, and the list is the feedback loop that proves the button worked. ⚠ **CORRECTED 2026-08-21 (later): this entry was written as if the day ended here, and it did not.** It said `prod` was "38 behind `main` and carries THREE migrations it has never seen"; within minutes the four commits merged to `main` (`42fa112`) and `main` was released to `prod` as `ce10dfa` (19:14), **migrations included** — prod's tracker carries `tutor_record`, `tutor_record_retire_legacy` and `tutor_add_lookup`, and the backlog is cleared. ⭐ **Third stale status flag in two days.** The flag is the one line in an entry guaranteed to rot; the only thing that has worked is checking `git log origin/prod` at session start instead of trusting it. **NEXT = 1d**, the only sub-slice that changes what the public can see.](sessions/2026-08.md)
- [2026-08 — **2026-08-21 — the tutor nobody can create, and the table that names one:** branch `claude/server-setup-12ef87`, **4 commits, 7 files, NO code and NO migration** — the whole session is documentation. **Merged to `main`** (⚠ this line said "ON BRANCH, not merged" and was stale within the hour — the same trap the session opened by correcting). Sam asked what it would take to get the programme side ready for real tutors. ⭐⭐ **The answer is that there is no way to create a tutor at all** — all three role-writing paths hardcode `'STUDENT'` (`register`, `welcome`, `ensureStudentRole`), nothing in the repo ever inserts `TUTOR`, the three admin surfaces are 20-line placeholders, and prod's single `TUTOR` row was written by hand. RLS already permits the write, so it is a **missing surface, not a missing permission**. ⚠ `admin-management.md` documented an invite path that does not exist — following it produces a **student**. ⭐ **The design is Sam's call, not mine:** I proposed a per-request applications table; he asked why not the sibling product's `teacher_profiles` shape, and he was right — **a request table records the application and still leaves no row representing the tutor**, which is exactly why there is nowhere to hang approval, suspension or a plan. So `nclex_tutors`, one row per person, `public_profile` lifted in from `nclex_users`, and the role left in `nclex_user_roles` because **the table explains the tutorship and the role grants access**. ⭐ From MyTeacher, the load-bearing idea: **registration never grants the role**, which is why `/register` and `/welcome` need no changes at all. ⚠ **THREE THINGS I GOT WRONG, ALL CAUGHT IN CONVERSATION:** ① "slice 7 is next" — read from the **tail** of a period file whose line 3 says *Newest first*, so I reported the oldest entry, written two days before Sam cancelled slice 7; ② "status transitions go in the audit log" — they cannot, `action` is CHECK-constrained to created/updated, and correcting it made me propose an events table that was **a partial reintroduction of the per-request table Sam had just talked me out of**; ③ calling MyTeacher's `request_count` a bolted-on "scar" — Sam built it deliberately, and `access-request.html` proved it is a **user-facing feature** ("Request #3"), which improved the design. ⭐ **Two of my own proposals withdrawn mid-design**, both on one principle — **standing and money are different axes** — each killed by a concrete bug: `EXPIRED` as a status value (suspend → lapse → pay → **a suspended tutor teaching again**) and `plan_type`/`access_expires_at` columns (a second copy of what `nclex_subscriptions` already models). Same principle settled **admission ≠ plan assignment**: you cannot sell a plan before deciding to admit, so approval puts everyone on the free tier. **Tutor plans PARKED** — they are quota tiers and nothing in `nclex_products` expresses a quota; ⚠ the free tier breaks the cardinality since **every tutor is always on exactly one plan**; the downgrade question is named and revenue-share recorded as a live alternative. Settled too: suspension = **keep materials, stop new joins**, no cascade into enrolments; role-less applicants get their own page; re-application updates the row in place. ⚠ **Found next door:** a programme student tapping **Profile** gets `/no-access?need=bank` — an advert for a product she did not buy — and **no user of any audience can manage their account**; `/tutor/students` is a reachable dead end; `bankAccessForUser`'s `TRIAL` arm is structurally impossible. ⓘ Ops: **Turbopack on Next 16.2.4 will not resolve `next` from a worktree without its own install** (the previous note is wrong for this version), and the tutor phone sweep **is** on `main` — that "not merged" flag was stale. **NEXT = BUILD, slice 1.**](sessions/2026-08.md)
- [2026-08 — **2026-08-20 (evening) — the tutor phone sweep: eleven surfaces, and four things a tutor could not do:** branch `claude/server-restart-bf7c36`, **12 commits, 15 files, NO migration** — app-layer, 12 of the 15 are stylesheets. **Merged to `main`** (`4dc4c30`..`30cd1b5`) — ⚠ **not on prod.** The job the previous entry ended by naming. Bank area excluded by Sam; Library skipped again, so it is now deferred on **both** audiences rather than half-done on either. ⭐⭐ **Four things a tutor simply could not do on a phone:** see whether a student had **started** (Status + Last active CLIPPED by an `overflow: hidden` card, 475px inside 341) · **get back to her programme list** (all seven drawer rows point deeper into the same programme) · reach cohort **Settings** (fifth tab at x364-443, past the edge, on a row that does not scroll) · **reorder or delete an activity** (`.act-controls` is `opacity: 0` until `:hover`, and a phone has no hover — present, hittable, invisible; **a hover-only affordance is not a small target, it is a missing one**). ⭐ Plus Sam's two, both found by USING the product: **Reply** sat off-screen at x378-437, and tapping an enquiry did nothing visible because the detail is 988px tall starting ~1600px down — **two screens** below the tap. ⭐⭐ **One cause, four times: `1fr` is `minmax(auto, 1fr)` and that `auto` floor is min-content, so the track cannot shrink** — and **twice it sat INSIDE a mobile block written to rescue narrow screens**, where the programmes override to `1fr` actually removed the fixed 340px minimum that had been CAPPING the damage (782px column inside 343, the sweep's worst number). **An existing breakpoint is not evidence a surface was ever measured.** ⚠ **Payments then taught the opposite** — it looked like a fifth sighting and was not: `minmax(0,1fr)` and `min-width: 0` both did nothing, and the real cause was `.tpay-page` having `margin: 0 auto` but no `width`, so in a COLUMN flex it sized to its **widest child** (633px inside 375) — a failure `styles/cat.css` already carries a written comment about. **Reaching for the trap you have seen four times is not diagnosing this one.** ⭐⭐ **`MobileNav` has ALWAYS had the slot for the missing back link** — `drawerHeader`'s own comment says "Programme/cohort pass name + Switch programme" and **no caller had ever passed it**; the doc deferred it as "polish" on reasoning true of the STUDENT surface only, so a deferral judged on one audience removed the other's only way out. ⭐ **Enquiries drill-in built** (behaviour change, discussed before code): at **900px not 768**, inside the existing stacking block, because the drill-in must begin exactly where the stacking does; ⚠ swapping the panes was **not enough** — 499px of stats+toolbar sit above the split, so it scrolls on both transitions, skipping the mount run. ⭐⭐ **Rule refined: if a chip strip can WRAP, SIZE the controls — do not grow invisible areas into a 4px gap** (the quiz badges' `::after` worked on the card surface and collided on the programme one; the card was one long tag name from the same fault at 302-inside-302). ⚠ **The instrument lied five distinct ways** — off-screen elements report a false pass, closed `opacity: 0` popovers inflate scrollWidth, hit tests during an animation fail, a labelled input's real target is its `<label>` (220x44, not 178x20), and an element under the fixed topbar reads as an overlap — and two working features were nearly "fixed" because of it. ⚠ Extending a hit area outward invents an overflow (done twice, one commit apart); ⚠⚠ `.prog-switch::after` is the switch's **KNOB**, so its hit area had to use `::before`. **Verified:** all nine live surfaces measure **sideways = 0**, re-run after every file was in place since `quiz.css`/`programmes.css` are shared; desktop unchanged throughout; `tsc` at the known `scoring-roundtrip` errors; `lint:check` clean at 30 known. ⬜ **NOT built:** Library (57px content pane, both audiences) · the bank area · three "Coming soon" placeholders · ⬜ `.ti-stats` at 335px puts half a screen of KPIs between the tutor and her queue (layout judgement, not a bug) · ⚠ the inner scroll container still open from the student sweep. **NEXT: Sam tests, then `main`.**](sessions/2026-08.md)

- [2026-08 — **2026-08-20 (later still) — the phone sweep: seven student surfaces, and two features that did not work:** branch `claude/work-session-ef6b2c`, **10 commits, 13 files, NO migration** — app-layer, 11 of the 13 are stylesheets. Sam asked what it would take to make the programme tutor-ready, heard the answer, then picked a different job: make the student surfaces work on a phone. Every surface driven at **375x812** as a real student and **measured**, not screenshotted — overflow, elements wider than the viewport, and every control under the 44px target. ⭐⭐ **The method missed the worst defect, which is the lesson: Quiz History reported a CLEAN `scrollWidth` of 375 while three of its seven columns were being destroyed.** A 652px table inside a 341px `overflow: hidden` card — the card swallows the excess, so an overflow test passes while Result (`73% · Pass`), State and the **Review link** are CLIPPED with no gesture that reaches them. A student could not see her own score or open her own review. Only measuring cell positions against the container edge found it; the fix already existed on `.hist-bank` and the programme table had simply never been added to it. ⭐ **Five surfaces, one bug in different costumes — a flex row whose metadata could not shrink while its content could:** week list title 118px vs a 121px progress bar · switcher popup with **64 elements past the panel edge** and the cohort name crushed to five stacked words · activity head title 91px against 182px of pills · sessions row title 138–177px of 343 · quizzes body 160px against a 133px action column. ⭐⭐ **Sam's catch changed the method** — the week list was truncating all 52 titles on **DESKTOP too, and worse** (a 7px bar holding 322px against the title's 118), so that fix moved into the BASE rule and desktop was measured **first** on every surface after; the answer was different each time, which is why checking beat assuming. ⭐ **The shared mobile chrome was itself the smallest thing on any screen** (pills 25px, sheet close 30px, avatar 34px, drawer 40px) — one file, every authenticated surface. **Rule settled: GROW the control where growing it is invisible; EXTEND THE HIT AREA where the control size IS the design** — and ⚠ **a hit area you cannot see is a fix you must prove**, so every one was verified with `elementFromPoint` probing OUTSIDE the visual box. ⚠ **One flag raised then withdrawn:** month-view chips were called too small at 25px, then measured at **262px wide with a 68px gap** and cleared — Sam pushed back and was right; **applying the number without checking the spacing was the error**. ⭐ Also: a self-paced student was told **"← Weeks"** about her modules, a string only a phone could ever render (`.scp-back` is `display: none` on desktop), now derived from the rail labels with `+ 's'` checked against `CHECK (unit_label IN ('WEEK','MODULE'))`. Self-paced routes walked separately from cohort ones, so "every student surface except Library" is verified, not inferred. ⬜ **NOT built:** Library (skipped) · ⚠ the **inner scroll container** (`.shell-root` is `height: 100dvh`, so the document never scrolls and the phone address bar never collapses — ~60–100px lost on every page; shell-level, its own session) · a `Note.In-progress` missing space that may be a Turbopack-vs-webpack artefact and wants a prod-build check first. **NEXT: the same sweep on the TUTOR side.**](sessions/2026-08.md)

- [2026-08 — **2026-08-20 (later) — the backlog got a number, then a gate:** branch `claude/work-session-8ecd14`, **4 commits, NO migration**, merged to `main` — ⚠ **not on prod**, and the only commits `prod` lacks. ⭐ **The session existed because the previous entry's own footnote asked for it** (`ⓘ npm run lint shows 47 errors repo-wide… worth its own look`) — nothing else pointed here. **47 → 30**, in two halves. ⭐ **Ten were never about the app** — `.claude/` tooling the linter was never meant to read (CommonJS helpers run by `node`, never bundled, and outside the `eslint app components lib` scope every session actually ran), a `require` in `next.config.ts` that is conditional **on purpose** (an `import` is hoisted, so the dev-only Cloudflare shim would reach production builds), and two `any`s tsc has **already refused** the honest type for — so **a lint error is not automatically a defect**. ⭐⭐ **But reading the rest found two real bugs, and neither had or could have had a test** — both are about *when React re-runs code*, not what it produces: `AddControl` was declared **inside** its parent, so React saw a new component **type** every render and remounted it, **collapsing the tutor-library Add menu mid-interaction**; and a comment claiming the live-session status was "snapshotted when the modal opens" was **false** — `Date.now()` sat in the render body, so Upcoming / Happening now / Ended could change under a student who had only **resized her window**. **A lint backlog is a defect queue nobody was reading.** ⭐ **`npm run lint:check` now answers the only question that matters day to day — "is this one mine?"** — comparing against `.eslint-baseline.json` and failing **only** on counts that went up. It counts **per file per rule, never pinned line numbers**: lines move on every edit, so a line-based baseline cries wolf on ordinary work and is ignored within a week — the exact failure it exists to end. ⭐⭐ **The June warning was discredited by its own prediction** — it said the errors "would bite a release build", but nothing runs lint in CI and Next 16 dropped the lint step inside `next build`, so the one stated reason to care never materialised and a correct warning died with its wrong consequence. ⚠ **CLAUDE.md carries the rule but deliberately NOT the number** — a count in prose goes stale the first time someone fixes something. ⭐ **`.githooks/pre-commit` now runs it, on STAGED FILES only, and the reason is a measurement:** whole-repo takes **71 s**, and a 71-second hook is one people learn to `--no-verify` past — **a bypassed gate is worse than no gate, because it trains the bypass**. Staged-only costs ~4 s and gives up nothing, since these rules are file-local. ⚠ Staged mode must **not** report the untouched backlog as fixed (skipped), and ⚠ **renames trip a false alarm** — the baseline is keyed by path, so a move reads "old path fixed, new path new"; re-baseline as part of the rename commit. ⭐ **The remaining 30 are one shape, not debris** — `react-hooks/refs` **17**, `set-state-in-effect` **11**, `immutability` **2** across 20 files, i.e. one themed session, in the same family that just produced both real defects. ⓘ The Supabase invite template is now unblocked (it waited on "only after prod", and the swap shipped in the release immediately before).](sessions/2026-08.md)

- [2026-08 — **2026-08-20 — the invite swap finished, and the live-class reminder built:** branch `claude/work-session-653b5d`, **8 commits, ONE migration** (`20260912120000_session_reminders.sql`, dev-applied), merged to `main`. Two arcs, both started by Sam noticing something. ⭐ **Pay-first invite swap** — the last `inviteUserByEmail` in the repo; a guest buying **bank, readiness or a self-serve programme** now gets **one** email carrying the setup link instead of two, closing a gap open since 08-11. ⚠ The swap was per **code path**, not per product, which is why it read as "bank is broken, programme is fine". ⭐⭐ **The duplication's birthday was the arrival of the GOOD email** — adding the receipt beside Supabase's invite is what made two; shipping the better half of a pair first *creates* the problem it exists to solve. ⭐⭐ **`auth.users.confirmation_sent_at` tells you which code an environment is running** (null = swap live), which settled the one test that looked broken — it had run against the deployed Worker. ⭐⭐ **`session.reminder` BUILT** — the product's **first fan-out and first attachment**: a 07:00 pass emails anyone with a class inside 7 days, carrying an **`.ics`** so her own phone reminds her thereafter, plus a tutor button capped at **one per class occurrence**. **The fingerprint (`session_id@epoch`) does four jobs at once** — per-student rows, automatic re-send on a reschedule, the cap, and the cap refilling when the class moves. ⚠ **Its own cron job, against the plan doc** — the enrolment sweep is one transaction that also *pauses students*, so a calendar bug would roll the pauses back. ⚠ **Four fixes to Claude's own work, every one from a Sam question rather than a test** (tsc/eslint green throughout): "queued 0" meant two opposite things; the switch was missing from `CONFIG_DEFS`; the tutor button ignored it; 02:15 → 07:00. ⭐ **A switch with two meanings** — editorial vs "this environment cannot render this yet" — cannot exempt the manual button without letting it report a send that will never happen. ⚠ `session_reminders_enabled` is **OFF on dev until deployed**.](sessions/2026-08.md)

- [2026-08 — **2026-08-19 (later) — three emails built, three shipped promises found broken:** branch `claude/work-session-067345`, **8 commits, 12 files, NO migration** — NOT on `main`. Built **`payment.tutor_received`**, **`enrolment.approved`**, **`enrolment.rejected`**. ⭐⭐ **The suppression rule — the recipient must not be the actor:** the catalog said tutor_received fires on both anchors, which would email a tutor a payment they typed in thirty seconds earlier; the test is **who recorded it** (`recorded_by_user_id === tutor_id` → silent), read **off the row** so no anchor can forget it — and it still sends when a **SUPER_ADMIN** records on someone else's programme. Proven both ways on dev (16 send / 1 admin-send / 2 suppress). ⚠ **Three defects already ON PROD, none found by tsc, eslint or tests — all three by reading real output:** ① a **`PAUSED` student's receipt said "Enrolled in"** while she was locked out (`result.ts` branched on PENDING_APPROVAL and let every other status fall through) — behaviour is right and unchanged, wording was wrong, and it needs **one** day of pause not the 74 the test row had, since the live nightly sweep pauses people on prod every night; ② **we promised an email that did not exist** — the receipt has told buyers since 08-18 that another email comes when the tutor approves, with **five dev enrolments** sitting told-so, a gap created when the 08-10 `enrolment.confirmed` fold covered only the ACTIVATED path and not the human `PENDING_APPROVAL` one; ③ a **double em-dash subject** whose guard comment already claimed to have solved it by counting only **our** dashes while the title supplies its own. ⭐ Rules banked: *when an email's copy promises another email, that promise is a catalog entry*; and *a subject interpolating a typed name must read as one sentence around it — zero separators of ours*. ⭐⭐ The rejection carries **the tutor's real email/phone**, replacing a Contact-tutor-form button that could **silently swallow her message** (`nclex_submit_enquiry` is idempotent on (programme,email) and returns the open lead without inserting, while showing a success tick) — ⚠ a live defect for **every repeat enquirer**. ⚠ No refund is promised because nothing refunds her; the tutor note is **not** sent (nothing has ever displayed it, so no tutor knows a student might read it); **no tutor has a phone number** and no screen collects one, so that row is conditional. Plus "Quademia admin" not QAcademy — ⚠ **16 more user-facing "QAcademy" strings** deferred, of which **2 are the copyright line and a legal-name question, not branding**. ⭐⭐ **Closed by designing the live-session emails (NOT built), where the shape moved four times and three of the four looked fine:** the version we nearly built sent the calendar invite **when the tutor schedules**, which Sam killed with one question — a student joining two weeks later gets nothing, and that is the NORMAL case since tutors set the schedule *before anyone enrols*, so it would have fanned out to an **empty cohort**. Settled on **a nightly pass — "which sessions fall in the next 7 days, and who hasn't been told" — which REPLACES all five event triggers rather than adding to them** (no door to forget, so the cron version is *simpler* than the event version), carrying an **`.ics` attachment** so her own phone reminds her thereafter, plus a tutor's manual button for the same-day case. ⭐ The outbox fingerprint does the "already told" bookkeeping, so unlike the instalment reminders the window need not tile — everything self-corrects on the next run. ⚠ Claude twice called the clock "missing"; the nightly sweep already runs and already enqueues, and only the dropped T-1h needed anything new.](sessions/2026-08.md)
- [2026-08 — **2026-08-19 — we own the redirect, and Google names the company — RELEASED TO PROD:** branch `claude/work-session-608e3a`, **one commit (`9480328`), NO migration** — app code only; PR [#54](https://github.com/QAcademy-Nurses/mynclex/pull/54), both workflows green, tracker untouched at **159** (pre-check clean both directions). **Build-order item ⑤ closed** — the identity arc's last open item. The app now builds Google's authorize URL itself (state, PKCE S256, `prompt=select_account`), catches her at our own `app/auth/google/callback`, exchanges the code server-to-server and hands the ID token to Supabase via `signInWithIdToken`; old `app/auth/callback` **deleted**. ⭐⭐ **Google shows the REGISTRABLE domain, not the hostname** — Sam spotted prod reading **"to continue to quademia.com"** (the `nclex.` dropped), and dev confirmed the rule from the other side (`mynclex-dev.mybackpacc.workers.dev` → `mybackpacc.workers.dev`). **So every future QAcademy product on a `quademia.com` subdomain inherits the same screen for free** — one company brand, no logo, no privacy policy, no verification queue. ⚠ **Two library contracts read in the installed source, not assumed**: **no `nonce` is sent** (auth-js compares the SHA-256 **hash** — the Apple shape — so Google's verbatim nonce can never match; state + PKCE already cover this flow) and **`access_token` IS sent** (Google's tokens carry `at_hash`). ⭐ **The refusal changed messenger, not behaviour** — the before-user-created hook now surfaces as a failed `signInWithIdToken`; proven on dev *and* prod: `GOOGLE_LOGIN_OK` with a fresh `last_sign_in_at`, `GOOGLE_BLOCKED`·`no_account` for the stranger, **`auth.users` +0 rows**, identities still dated 08-09 (linking reused, no duplicate). ⚠⚠ **Trap of the night: a dashboard "Variable" is DELETED by the next deploy** — both values were set as Variables and `wrangler deploy` wiped them three minutes later; **Secrets survive, Variables don't**. Now in CLAUDE.md → *Known Workarounds*. ⭐ The distinct `google_unavailable` answer diagnosed it from outside with no Cloudflare access — same shape as the 503-vs-401 split the night before — and prod's were corrected **before** the release. ⓘ **Client topology settled**: one OAuth client per ENVIRONMENT (dev = personal account, prod = workspace), matching every other vendor; Claude argued for consolidating both into the workspace project twice and was wrong twice. NEXT: `payment.tutor_received` · identity switch + full-access Resend key · stuck-queue alarm.](sessions/2026-08.md)

Pre-check clean both directions (155 ↔ 155). Three hand-set values: Vault secret (Sam, dashboard — 64 chars, value never in a transcript), `email_drain_url`, and `CRON_SECRET` on the prod Worker — ⚠ **which fought back**: the first add never landed and the door said 503 for half an hour. ⭐ **The 503-vs-401 split earned its keep** — "no secret configured" vs "wrong key" diagnosed from outside with `curl` alone; delete + re-add fixed it (likely the dashboard's save-without-deploy draft trap). ⭐ **Unattended proof on prod**: knocks 19:45–20:00 all 503, the 20:05 knock **200 `{claimed 0}`** — cron → Vault → pg_net → Worker → auth → drain, no hand. ⭐⭐ **Sam's test beat mine**: instead of a synthetic SQL row, a **real GHS 120 bank purchase** (Paystack test mode) — receipt `SENT` in **218 ms** via the ⚡ path, attempt 1, provider id, **the first email ever through prod's Resend key**. - [2026-08 — **2026-08-18 — the drain, and the sweep that stopped being silent — BUILT:** branch `claude/work-session-72d3ec`, **10 commits, 3 migrations**, all merged to `main` + deployed to dev. Nothing student-visible. ⭐⭐ **The headline was not 1b.** Building the drain found that the **retry policy settled on 08-11 had never run once** — a failed row gets a future `send_after` and nothing ever re-read it, so the only second attempt in the product was a human pressing Retry. The drain repairs the ⚡ emails already on `main`, independent of anything scheduled. ⭐ **Sam proposed a listener** (Supabase Database Webhooks); real, stronger than pitched — it reopens 1a's rejection of queue-and-flush, which was aimed at *polling* latency — but **parked**: it cannot hear retries, rows queued ahead of their time, or a ring that fails, and its only edge is latency, which is worthless for ⏰. ⭐ **Doorbell = pg_cron, not GitHub Actions**, decided by the retry delays (1/5/15/30 min): Actions floors at 5 min and bills whole minutes → ~8,600/month against a 2,000 allowance; Cloudflare cron is unavailable because OpenNext 1.19.6 emits no scheduled handler. ⚠ **An hourly Actions schedule was nearly shipped** and would have stretched "five attempts in an hour" to four hours. ⚠ **Doc correction:** pg_cron *can* call out — pg_net is available, just off; the wall is **rendering**, not HTTP. ⚠ **Caught by Sam:** the new config row had no switch, because `/admin/config` renders `CONFIG_DEFS` not the table. ⭐ **Proven unattended** — queued 11:31:38, the 11:30 knock said `claimed 0`, the 11:35 knock said `claimed 1, sent 1`. ⭐⭐ **THEN SLICE 1b**: `payment.installment_due` (T-7 + T-3) and `payment.installment_overdue` — **the first time-driven emails the product has ever sent**, the half gamma designed and never built. The due-date sum is now written ONCE (`nclex_enrolment_next_payment()`), which REMOVED two inline copies rather than adding a fourth. Copy: past tense (detecting overdue and pausing are the same instant, so "you will be paused" is never true) · "paying puts it back immediately", checked in activate.ts before writing it · the tutor is named · consequence lines conditional, because non-gated programmes are never paused · grace-covered students get no overdue email at all · Sam's subject label `Access paused: <programme>`, with the repeating tail cut so the programme survives a phone's truncation (82→57 chars). ⚠⚠ **Two process findings worth more than the feature:** the migration FILE was not what was tested (hash-compared, cosmetic this time) and **`tsc` proves nothing while `next dev` runs** — half-written `.next/dev/types` files cause syntax errors, so tsc skips semantic checks and hid two real errors. ✅ **Fixed:** the quota/attempts gap — two counters now, because the one-line fix breaks CONFIG's back-off. ⚠→✅ **Found AND fixed the same session:** a bad API key comes back as `validation_error` → PERMANENT → DEAD on the FIRST failure, so a bad-key day would have had the drain kill the queue row by row while the sweep refilled it. ⭐ Building the drain promoted a dormant misclassification into an active one. Fix = **401 → CONFIG, checked before the code table** (probed live: malformed, wrong and empty keys all return the identical 401/validation_error); `validation_error` keeps PERMANENT everywhere else, since it is also the real code for a genuinely broken request. ⚠ **Prod has none of the email arc.**](sessions/2026-08.md)

- [2026-08 — **2026-08-12 — the email arc: the tutor enrols you, and the resend we discovered we did not owe — BUILT:** branch `claude/work-session-45d385`, **3 commits, NO migration**, merged to `main`.
