Consequence, agreed: **wire a feature's emails while already inside that feature's code**; ⚠ except the clock, which nothing will ever drag in — exactly how gamma got a working event half and a time half never built. ⚠ Corollary Sam's version missed: "as we go" only catches features we RETURN to, so payments/enrolments/enquiries emails must be *chosen*. **Built:** ⭐ **two dials, not four emails** (dial 1 `reason` = wording, dial 2 `entry` = the work; neither guessed — the function already returns `invited`) · two keys sharing ONE template, alias hidden from the preview list · ⭐ **the invite swap** (`inviteUserByEmail`→`generateLink`: Supabase mints the account and sends nothing, our email carries the link — one email, the rich one wins, third application) which ⚠ makes our email load-bearing · ⭐ **a queue failure now reaches the TUTOR** (deliberate opposite of the receipt's swallow — by then money has moved and nobody is there; here the tutor is looking at the screen and is the only one who can act), so the toast goes error-tone on a *successful* action · ⚠ **the send is the LAST line of `inviteOrAttachAndEnrol`** because the backfill 30 lines above DELETES the enrolment on failure — found by reading to the bottom, invisible to tsc · the money line is **disclosure not chasing**, else her first news of owing is the sweep pausing her. ⭐⭐ **The best outcome was work we did NOT do:** Sam asked what happens when the link expires — there is no resend, ⚠ and admin **Retry can never be one** (frozen payload → re-sends the same dead link, reports success). But `generateLink` creates the account at the tutor's click and `/login`'s "Email me a sign-in code" only asks that the account exist — **verified live** from exactly that state, so the resend was cancelled rather than built. ⚠ And `/welcome` had been saying *"Ask your tutor to add you again"* in two places, which the duplicate guard refuses — wrong since written; 3 copy fixes, Sam reframing the third ("you don't need it" was dismissive right after something failed). ⓘ The welcome card reassures about the ACCOUNT not the enrolment — convergence point, pay-first buyer has none yet. **Tested 4 of 6 variants live: 8 outbox rows, every one SENT first attempt**, incl. the null-cohort/null-expiry case and the part-paid waitlist convert (which proves the backfill ordering); Sam stopped there, the 2 skipped being recombinations of proven halves. ⚠⚠ **A lost test run was worth the hour** — the first attempt hit the **deployed dev Worker** (runs `main`, has none of this), and chasing it found **two silences in my own code**: a `{queued:false}` that said nothing, and a `.data?.title` collapsing *query failed* into *no title* — the same shape as the receipt's delivery column on 08-11. ⚠ Also: an `@example.com` recipient makes the success toast **lie** (declined unqueued, reported sent; all 3 seeded leads are example.com). ⏭ **NEXT = 1b**, Sam's reason being *it will teach us the other time-controlled emails*: the outbox likely needs **nothing** (`stage` already IS the already-warned ledger; T-24h/T-1h are two rows) — what may need a table is the **job**, since nothing records that a run happened; ⚠ and the 7 **do not share a clock** — payments/access ride the nightly sweep, `session.reminder` at T-1h cannot. Still open: how the send half is triggered, pg_cron being unable to reach Resend. ⚠ Nothing on `prod`.](sessions/2026-08.md)
- [2026-08 — **2026-08-11 — the email arc, slice 1a: the queue, and the receipt that proves it — BUILT:** on-machine, branch `claude/work-session-00c090`, **4 commits, one migration** (`20260908120000_email_outbox.sql`, dev-applied), **not on `main`**. Build-order item ⑥'s first code. Before today the app could not send a single email of its own: no `resend` package, no `lib/email/`, `workers/` holding one `.gitkeep` — someone paid and heard **nothing**. Half the session was design, one question at a time. ⭐ **Inline send, not queue-and-flush** — a receipt 5–15 min late is worse than one in 3 s, and no scheduler beats that; `waitUntil` (verified: `getCloudflareContext().ctx`, dev already wired) returns her page at once. ⭐⭐ **Sam killed my retry schedule and was right** — I proposed 5 tries across a day; *"a system that quietly succeeds on attempt four has hidden a day-long problem from you"*, so long automatic retry is a mechanism for **not finding out**, the exact thing this layer exists to prevent → ~1 hour, then a human. ⭐⭐ **His "no cron for retries" needed a correction, not a rejection** — I separated two jobs he'd merged: *retrying* (optional) vs *sending the scheduled ones at all* (**not** optional; pg_cron cannot reach Resend). Job B exists for 1b regardless, so retry is that same machine on its rounds — a policy, not a component. ⭐ **His "will the failure reason be tracked?" broke my design**: Resend names every rejection and they divide by what a retry could achieve, so **the reason decides, not a count** (can-never-work → DEAD on the *first* failure · hiccup → back off · quota → tomorrow · bad key → retry forever *and* alarm). ⚠ **There is no Resend error for a bad recipient** — a typo'd address is ACCEPTED and bounces later, never entering the retry loop, leaving as `SENT`; my "dead address clogs the list" worry was wrong in its mechanism. ⭐⭐ **"Could we skip the outbox and read Resend instead?"** — their read API is better than assumed (list · retrieve · `last_event` · body retained), so it got a real answer: four things rule it out, each fatal — it only knows what it ACCEPTED · duplicate protection must precede the network call · "who have I warned?" must run at scan speed · a failed send left nothing to retry from. **Our table owns intent+attempt, Resend owns outcome**, joined on `provider_message_id` — which is what let the bounce webhook be deferred. ⭐⭐ **"The catalog is a capture list, not a build plan"** (Sam) — some of the 24 will never be built, others will appear unlisted; so catch-up (send late or skip) stays **per-email** and `EmailEventKey` lists only what is wired. ⭐ **Dev sends real email**, and his argument beat mine: for a *mailer*, suppressing in dev makes "I tested it" meaningless. Data first — dev holds 36 users, 18 inert `@example.com`, 17 aliases of his own inbox, **2 real third parties**, and today's scan would reach exactly one enrolment (his). ⚠ One exception: `@example.com` declined unqueued (18 guaranteed hard bounces ≈ 90 % bounce rate on a new account). **Same domain dev+prod, keys separate them** (`mynclex-dev-app`/`-prod-app`, mirroring the SMTP pair) — he overruled my dev subdomain. **Rolling windows, never calendar dates** — general rule for all 7 ⏰ emails: "due today" is broken because the sweep looks once a day (a payment due 01:00 is never "today" at 02:00 before, and already overdue at 02:00 on the day); "within the next 24 h" tiles end-to-end with the run interval → **exactly one warning, always one full day before the pause**; ⚠ which makes the missed-run policy load-bearing, and **no interlock** (that would tie access enforcement to Resend's uptime). ⭐ **Reading the pay-first branch found a defect in the doc's own design** — the `enrolment.confirmed` fold assumed "same moment, same person"; false for a guest, since `activateGroup` marks `SETUP_REQUIRED` and creates **no enrolment, no subscription, no credits** until `/welcome` days later → **three framings**, with every grants line null under the last (a bank pass has no end date because `end_at` is computed *at* activation). The fold's *conclusion* survives for a different reason: she is **sitting in the app** at `/welcome`. And that framing is the **only message reaching her if the invite itself failed**. **Built:** 4 folders, 14 new files (2 beyond announced — `layout.tsx`+`actions.ts`, forced by admin-route convention), 8 changed; `lib/email/`, the migration, `/admin/emails` (stuck list) + `/preview` (every variant vs fixtures); reused `getPaymentReceipt` so screen and email cannot drift; **no `resend` SDK** (plain fetch — node_modules lives in the parent and `npm install` here breaks the dev CSS). ⭐ **Five defects, every one found by reading OUTPUT, none by tsc/eslint/types**: "Enrolled in **Payment 2 of 4**" (cohort read from the line, but `PROGRAMME_INSTALLMENT` carries `cohort_id NULL` by the `cohort_scope` CHECK) · a two-em-dash subject · ACTIVATED payments with no enrolment/subscription link silently dropping the whole "what you now have" section · his `noreply@` call exposing a footer that said **"Reply to this email"** (fixed both ways: copy points at `support@`, plus a **Reply-To header** for those who never read it) · and his **send-only key**, which sent perfectly and returned `restricted_api_key` on every read — `fetchDeliveryStatus` swallowed that and returned `{}`, making *"Resend hasn't said"* identical to *"we couldn't ask"*, **the exact silence this layer exists to detect, reproduced inside the monitor**. **Proven on dev:** index refuses a duplicate + admits a different stage · 7 variants render clean, GHS voice intact · real joins ("Enrolled in Q3 Upcoming Cohort · GHS 2,000 remaining, next due 6 June 2026") · twice-called leaves ONE row · **Sam pressed Retry and a real receipt sent** (GHS 1,700, real message id). ⚠⚠ **Two mistakes of mine:** I corrupted `.env.local`'s encoding (PowerShell ANSI read → UTF-8 write, mangling em-dashes and ⚠ in *pre-existing* Turnstile/Paystack comments too) — repaired via cp1252 reversal, BOM stripped, both copies byte-identical, 8 keys intact, `/login` re-verified; and **I claimed `CLAUDE.md` had a stale email-worker line 4–5 times without opening it** — it was corrected on 08-10, and the stale thing was **the doc's note about CLAUDE.md**, the same dated-claim trap as session logs saying "not on prod". ⏭ NEXT = **1b (the clock + `payment.installment_due`)** — Sam may do something else email-related first, but 1b must not drift, being the half that historically never gets built; then `payment.tutor_received`, the alarm, the invite swap.](sessions/2026-08.md)
- [2026-08 — **2026-08-10 (later) — the email arc, planned: two builds wearing one list — DESIGN ONLY, no code:** session branch `claude/work-session-d22bab`. Build-order item ⑥. **Zero code; two doc files.** What exists today is **nothing** — no `resend` package, no `lib/email/`, `workers/` holds one `.gitkeep`; the live Resend+SMTP sends **Supabase's** identity mail, not ours. ⭐⭐ **Sam's first question reframed the arc** — *"is it payments alone or all forms of emails except custom SMTP?"* Neither: SMTP is **plumbing, not a category** (both layers use the same wire since 08-06); the real split is **who composes** — Supabase sees an address and a link and nothing else, which is exactly why the invite can only say *you have an account*, never *for what*. ⭐⭐ **The catalog was two builds wearing one list, and Sam saw it** (*"if I paid for a product the email I get is different from the first reminder that my subscription expires in 7 days"*): **⚡ event-driven (17)** vs **⏰ time-driven (7)** — they differ in more than timing (late means opposite things; double-send risk is a retry vs a **table scan**; a missed run needs a policy; time-driven is a **series**). I had blurred it by arguing the outbox on "pg_cron can enqueue" — the correction is his. ⭐⭐ **Gamma proves the split empirically** (read-only survey, Sam's call): its event half works (6 events, ~180 lines, `EVENT_MAP` + `{{placeholder}}` + shared footer) but its **time half was never built** — `expiry_reminded` written **`false` in 7 places, `true` in 0**, no cron anywhere, and `{{expiryDate}}`/`{{renewUrl}}` placeholders **no template uses**. Not because it was hard: that stack has **nowhere to run a clock**. ⭐ **No email Worker** (Sam: *"we don't need one here because we have servers"*) — and gamma's **fails at the job it exists for**: `EMAIL_SECRET`, the secret needed to reach it, ships to every browser in `js/config.js`, so its auth check is decoration; ⚠ **flagged as a live gamma-prod issue** (branded mail from a verified domain → sending reputation, ~629 users), untouched. `CLAUDE.md`'s worker line corrected. ⚠ **The marker convention never happened** — **one** `EMAIL-TRIGGER` in the codebase and its key isn't in the catalog, so *"markers ARE the wiring points"* is a promise the repo can't keep. ⭐⭐ **"We have several types of payments" caught a real defect in my design** — I'd keyed the receipt on `payment_id`, but `checkout_group_id` groups one charge's rows and `paystack_reference` is shared, so a programme+bank checkout would send **two receipts for one debit** — the exact "charged twice?" alarm the key exists to prevent. **The receipt is per CHECKOUT**; the five purposes are **line items**. ⭐ **`enrolment.confirmed` folded into `payment.received`** — same moment, same person, same checkout; **the same duplication Sam caught on 08-06 with the invite**, same rule (*one email, the rich one wins*); ⚠ pay-first (`SETUP_REQUIRED`) branch unread. **Fingerprint** = email · subject · stage, enforced by the **database** not check-then-insert (Paystack retries create the race); ⚠ **too tight is worse than too loose** — nobody reports an email they didn't know was coming. **First pair:** ⚡ `payment.received` + ⏰ `payment.installment_due` — ⭐ the scheduled pick changed on reading code: the 02:00 sweep **already computes the due date**, and that same expression **pauses access**, so re-deriving it risks *"access paused"* reaching somebody **never warned**. **Templates** = one `.ts` file per event (not `.html` — bundler config in two bundlers, and this repo's scars are exactly there), ⭐ carrying a **SNAPSHOT not a lookup** (precedent: `strategy_snapshot_json`). **Monitoring** (Sam asked first) = "stuck rows" page + alert; ⚠ **Resend's dashboard can't see it** (a dead sender looks like a quiet week) and ⚠ **the alarm shares the failure it alarms about**. Folders agreed, none created. **⏭ Next: build slice 1.**](sessions/2026-08.md)
- [2026-08 — **2026-08-10 — the company gets an address of its own — A SECOND REPO, LIVE ON PROD:** on-machine, branch `claude/work-session-4c41fc`. **Almost none of this session is in this repo.** It created **`QAcademy-Nurses/quademia-parent-site`** (new, private) and released it — **`quademia.com` + `www` now serve a real site**, with `/privacy` and `/terms` as *Coming soon* placeholders; 4 commits there, both branches in sync. Here: doc corrections + a one-line pin, nothing shipped. ⭐⭐ **Sam reversed yesterday's decision and was right.** 08-09 had settled that the legal pages live in THIS repo at `nclex.quademia.com/privacy` "for now"; he asked *why do something now and redo it later?* Three lines fell: ⭐⭐ **the cost was mis-priced, not mis-judged** — the doc costed a *website* ("new repo, build, Worker/Pages project, DNS and deploy pipeline, from zero") when he had asked for **two pages**, and the whole thing took one session; ⭐ **"moving later is cheap" was the weakest line on the page** — a privacy URL is *recorded by outsiders* (Google's consent config, Turnstile's addendum requirement, the payment provider), so moving means re-telling each and possibly re-running brand verification, and "for now" would never end, leaving a **MyTeacher** user reading the *company's* policy on a *nursing product's* subdomain; ⭐ **the extraction rule applies in reverse** — legal pages here don't break `cp -r mynclex/` going *out*, they break it coming *back*, and the precedent is our own (`mynclex` was a folder in gamma, went stale, cut out 2026-05-19). ⓵ **A third option was designed and rejected**: serving `quademia.com/privacy` off THIS Worker via zone routes needs **three** special cases — asset carve-out (or `/_next/*` is swallowed and the page arrives unstyled), login-cookie scoping guard (an apex Supabase cookie reaches every subdomain), middleware exclusion — all three existing only because one app would answer to two identities. **When the clever option needs three special cases and the plain one needs none, the clever option is only cheaper this week.** ⭐ **Two more Sam calls, both against my recommendation:** *same stack, not static files* (his: we don't know what it becomes — and the ceiling was already in our build order as **item ⑦ cross-product SSO**, which HTML can never grow into; plus one stack = one thing to understand), and *two branches, not one* (I argued a single environment since there's no database, no accounts, no migrations; he overruled — **a release routine is something he performs, and performing two across the family is the cost that lands**). Both arguments kept in that repo's `CLAUDE.md` so nobody simplifies it back. Verified in order — localhost → dev workers.dev → prod workers.dev → domain **last**, so a half-built site was never answering at the company's address. ⭐ **The apex-is-dark problem closed as a side effect**, better than the planned redirect. ⚠⚠ **THE FINDING THAT RE-ORDERS TWO LISTS: a privacy policy must name a data controller and Quademia Ltd is NOT incorporated** — so ORC registration is **upstream** of the legal text, not parallel; the parent site carries no "Ltd" on any page as a standing rule. ⚠ Traps: **`@opennextjs/cloudflare` must be pinned exactly** (`^1.19.1` floats to 1.20.x which needs next ≥16.2.11 vs our 16.2.4 — `npm install` fails outright; **this repo had the same bug**, hidden by its lockfile, now pinned to `1.19.6`); **Next 16.2.4 carries 22 advisories** incl. a Middleware/Proxy bypass on an app whose auth guards ARE middleware (fix needs one coordinated bump of both repos — flagged, not done); the **first request to a route within ~30 s of a deploy can 404** during edge rollout; **a negative DNS answer outlives the fix** (apex kept failing locally for ~an hour after going live worldwide, `flushdns` useless — the cache was upstream); and **`workers_dev` + `preview_urls` both change themselves** when `routes` appears, now stated explicitly. ⚠ A **stale 07-30 note** had me open the session listing "the runner is broken at phone widths" — the mobile arc closed 08-03; checked before recommending it, which is the only reason it didn't become a session.](sessions/2026-08.md)

- [2026-08 — **2026-08-09 (later still) — Google becomes a sign-in method, not a sign-up method — RELEASED TO PROD:** on-machine, branch `claude/server-startup-4afebc`, **3 commits, one migration** (`20260907120000_google_signin.sql`). Build-order item ⑤ **built, proven on dev, released (PR #52, tracker 154 → 155), and proven again on prod** — refusal, registration, linking and sign-in each driven by Sam against a real Google account on both. ⭐ **The planned hold was lifted by separating cosmetics from function**, not by overriding it: the raw `dehspjcfmhoshcdtsmjq.supabase.co` consent screen is accepted for now, because what mattered was proving the flow works on prod. Prod after: 3 users, `profileless: 0`, **`google_orphans: 0`** — the refusal wrote nothing anywhere. ✅ **The prod app is PUBLISHED**, so Google sign-in is live for real students — and ⭐ **publishing did NOT need the privacy policy**: `email`+`profile` are non-sensitive scopes, so the document gates *verification* (the branded screen), not publishing and not the feature. A day of "blocked on a missing page" reasoning was overtaken the same day it was written. ⓘ Left = the privacy policy (owed for Turnstile invisible mode, verification, and its own legal merits), branding, the invited-but-unfinished student, and ⑥. ⭐⭐ **Sam reversed the design and the plan had it backwards for four days** — the doc had said since 08-05 that Google creates first-time accounts; his point was that our platform needs a subscription or enrolment, so an empty account can do nothing and there is no point making them. Google verifies who she is; **we** verify she has an account with us. ⚠ **My first answer was wrong**: I argued sign-in-only meant create-then-delete and its lockout risk — but the `before-user-created` hook refuses *before* the row is written, so there is nothing to delete. Retraction recorded rather than the conclusion quietly swapped. ⭐ **The evidence that settled his second pushback** ("why does a hook come into it at all?"): both account-creating paths look the person up in `nclex_users` but create in `auth.users`, so a Google stranger's profile-less row makes `inviteUserByEmail` collide with the unique email index — the tutor cannot enrol her, and the payment path returns "we could not send the setup email", i.e. **she has paid and cannot get in**. ⭐ **The hook looks nothing up** — automatic linking means a *creation* via Google is itself proof no account exists, so the rule is `provider = 'google'` → refuse, and it structurally cannot shut `/register` or the invite paths. ⭐ **Sam's naming call — retire, don't repurpose**: `GOOGLE_FIRST_SIGNIN` → **`GOOGLE_LOGIN_OK` + `GOOGLE_BLOCKED`**, the pair declining a fourth success-type-without-a-refusal (the last two cost a migration each). **Hook proven live** by a sentinel probe against the real signup endpoint (403, our own words) after a first probe proved inconclusive; ⚠ a redirect-allowlist test was **reported as unproven** when its control (`evil.example.com`) passed identically. ⭐⭐ **Sam's three tests contained an accident that proved the whole thesis**: refused at 21:30:22, registered 32 seconds later — without the hook that registration would have failed "already registered", locking him out of an account he never created. One new user in three hours, zero google orphans, linking confirmed (`identities: email + google`, no duplicate). Settled: **the consent screen is per-project, not per-client**, so one `Quademia` project with a client per product — ⚠ but **not** by renaming gamma's project, which is the live login path for ~629 real users. ⚠ Scope I invented and withdrew: an env-var gate to ship the button dark, dropped after Sam asked why we weren't just merging — it was code written to avoid waiting.](sessions/2026-08.md)

- [2026-08 — **2026-08-09 — the door Turnstile closed on Claude, then slice 3: email-code login — MERGED to `main`:** on-machine, branch `claude/turnstile-verification-crash-6aed4f`, **9 commits**, ONE migration (`20260906120000_code_blocked.sql`, dev-applied); vitest **948 → 952**, eslint clean, tsc at the known errors. **Build-order item ③ BUILT + verified on dev, NOT released.** **⭐ THE SESSION OPENED ON SAM'S CONSTRAINT AND IT WAS WORSE THAN STATED** — he warned that Turnstile crashes Claude's browser on the three auth pages (true, and **not fixable**: Turnstile exists to detect an automated browser, Claude's browser is one, so *the product working correctly is the failure mode*). Checking the env rather than accepting it found that **neither `.env.local` had any Turnstile keys**, dev's captcha was on, and therefore **every login, signup and reset on `localhost:3000` had been refused since 08-08 — for Sam too, silently**. ⚠ Cause: `.worktreeinclude` copies `.env*` **parent → child only**, so keys written inside the pruned 08-08 worktree died with it. **⭐ SAM ASKED FOR THE EXPLANATION TWICE AND THE SECOND ONE LANDED** (*"i still dont understand"*); he then reasoned the fix out himself — the auto-copy could carry the key every session, but a real key still locks Claude out — which is right, **and the two only conflict if the key is real**. Settled: **dev runs Cloudflare's published testing pair**, so the widget still renders and every step still runs (pass issued → forwarded → validated → answer read) with only the judgement stubbed. ⚠ **I offered a choice that was not one** — claimed the secret swap would break the deployed dev Worker; it would not (an always-pass secret waves real tokens through too), and the honest reason to change the committed files is that **the config was lying**, naming a real widget while the secret rubber-stamped everything. **⭐⭐ PROD WAS PROVEN, WHICH 08-08's CHECK HAD NOT DONE** — that probe sent NO token, which shows prod *demands* a pass; a door that asks for ID and never reads it answers identically. Re-probed with a **garbage** token, prod returned `captcha_failed — invalid-input-response`: **prod validates**. Docs: new CLAUDE.md Known Workaround (*the day dev goes back to real keys those pages hang again — check the keys first*), CLAUDE.md env section (was listing **three** of six required vars), and `domain-and-identity.md` layer 1, which **still asked for two checks of a single-use token** — the 08-08 correction had been noted lower on the page but never applied where the design is stated. **⭐ THEN SLICE 3, WHERE THREE PLANNED THINGS TURNED OUT WRONG AND EACH WAS CAUGHT BY CHECKING, NOT BY BUILDING:** ① *"no migration needed"* was 75% right — 2a foresaw the types describing what the STUDENT did and missed the one describing what WE do (refuse her), so `CODE_BLOCKED` cost a migration; ⏭ **slice 5 has the same shape** (`GOOGLE_FIRST_SIGNIN` has no refusal partner). ② *"shorten the OTP expiry to 10 minutes"* — **one dial moves every email token**, so it would have cut the password-reset link to ten minutes while its template promised an hour, hurting exactly the person that flow exists for, and buys almost nothing once a per-address rule exists (~30 guesses/hour against a million); ⚠ **I had recommended it and Sam had accepted it**, and withdrawing it on evidence was right. ③ *"slice 3 will meet the `?code=` trap"* — **it does not**; that trap needs a LINK carrying a token and a typed code never touches a URL. **⭐ THE HALF-BUILT ACCOUNT BECAME A MEASUREMENT, BY ACCIDENT** — the same call without `shouldCreateUser:false` created an `auth.users` row, already confirmed and signed in, with **zero** profile rows and **zero** roles; the 500 the caller sees arrives afterwards from the email, so the error is not even the damaging part. **The identical trap waits on slice 5's OAuth callback.** **⭐ THE COOKIE IS PART OF THE ENUMERATION SILENCE** — set on every path, because one set only for real accounts would answer through a side channel the question every line refuses to answer. **⭐ SAM'S OWN RUN IS THE END-TO-END PROOF**: `CODE_REQUESTED` → `CODE_LOGIN_OK` **39 seconds apart**, send genuinely succeeded, `last_login_utc` written. ⚠ **And I nearly missed it** — I had dismissed the two absent event ids as harmless IDENTITY gaps, then queried `event_id > 99`, got nothing, and was one step from reporting that his sign-in bypassed our code; **they were his login, concurrent with my testing**. *Explaining evidence away is not checking it.* Everything else driven and read off the table: unknown address indistinguishable · both thresholds bite with correct countdowns · blocks never feed the counters · reload does not strand her · cookie cleared server-side · `httpOnly` real · **375px clean** · ⭐ **the two counters proved separate in the live system** (a verify-blocked address could still request). ⚠ **Two bugs found only by driving the UI**: a **nested `<form>`** (browsers silently drop the inner one, so the control would have done nothing) and **React 19 resetting the field after every action** (wanted, but it made `required` block later submits before the action ran). ⚠ **Named, not hidden under "done"**: resend never clicked, Turnstile has never met a real challenge on this door (permanent consequence of the testing-pair decision), `next` unthreaded through the code door. ⬜ Prod release needs **template 3 pasted into prod's Magic Link template** first. ⏭ **Next = item ④**, attaching `nclex.quademia.com`; ⓘ item ⑤ (Google) carries a **multi-day external gate** (Google brand verification) worth starting early.](sessions/2026-08.md)

- [2026-08 — **2026-08-08 — slice 2d: Turnstile, and the discovery that the plan asked for something impossible — MERGED to `main`:** on-machine, branch `claude/work-session-4e2f66`, **5 commits**, ONE migration (`20260905120000_register_rejected.sql`, dev-applied); vitest **939 → 948**, eslint clean, tsc at the known errors. **Build-order item ② is COMPLETE.** **⭐⭐ THE HEADLINE IS A CORRECTION TO THE PLAN, NOT A FEATURE** — the doc asked for Turnstile *"verified server-side inside the server action … (use the native Supabase↔Turnstile integration so the direct endpoint also demands a token)"*, which is TWO checks, and a Turnstile token **can be validated exactly once** (`timeout-or-duplicate` on a replay). Step 1 shipped the first half, it worked, and it made the Supabase switch **impossible to turn on** — our call spent the token, so Supabase would have been handed a used one and refused every sign-in, signup and reset on the site. Only building it, then reading Cloudflare's actual rules, could surface that. **⭐ SUPABASE BECAME THE VERIFIER**, being the only one of the two standing at BOTH doors: the anon key is public by design, so anyone can call Supabase's auth endpoint directly and never touch our code. `verifyTurnstile` → `readTurnstileTicket` — no network call, answers only "is Turnstile on / did a token arrive" and forwards it in `options.captchaToken`. **⭐ PROVEN, NOT ASSUMED:** a direct POST with just the anon key now returns `captcha_failed / "no captcha_token found"`; yesterday it tried the password and walked past Turnstile **and** every 2c threshold. **⭐ SAM ASKED WHETHER OUR WORK WAS THEN CEREMONIAL** — no: Supabase's setting only DEMANDS a ticket, it never ISSUES one; the widget, the forms, the reset logic and the Worker secrets are what make the switch flippable at all, and what changed hands was one function call. He also asked if the widget could be invisible — technically yes, but Invisible has nowhere to put a challenge (this audience is disproportionately the visitor who needs one, same carrier-NAT reason that forbids a per-IP rule) and Cloudflare requires a Privacy Addendum reference in a privacy policy this product does not have. **⭐ A CAPTCHA REFUSAL IS NOT A FAILED PASSWORD** — Supabase reports it as an ordinary auth error, so unhandled it would feed 2c's counter and lock a student out of an account she typed correctly every time; routed to `*_BLOCKED` / `REGISTER_REJECTED` instead. **⭐ THE SINGLE-USE PASS WAS THE REAL DIFFICULTY** — a mistyped password spends hers before the screen says so, so every form resets on every failed submit; verified twice in the log as `LOGIN_FAIL` → `LOGIN_OK` with no block between, once under each verifier. **⭐ SAM'S TESTING FOUND WHAT NOTHING ELSE COULD:** a `RESET_BLOCKED / turnstile:missing_token` then success 12 s later — the form renders instantly, the pass takes a moment, and submitting in that gap is the app blaming her for being quick; ⚠ **the window is WIDER for this product's students, not narrower**. Submit now waits for the pass, with **three guards against a dead form** (no site key → ready at once; error/timeout **unlock** rather than lock; a 10 s backstop). tsc, eslint and 948 tests were green the whole time that bug was live. **Step 2** = `REGISTER_REJECTED` + the arc's only NOT-free event type, which also **reversed an earlier decision on purpose** — the rollback paths are logged now, because `REGISTER_REJECTED` can say "this signup did not happen" where `REGISTERED` could not, and it is the support case that matters most. ⚠ **I told Sam `localhost` was auto-allowed on a Turnstile widget; it is not** — error **110200**, no token, every sign-in refused. ⚠ The dev server died three times, each the documented `node_modules/.vite` Turbopack trap; ⭐ Sam's theory that Turnstile was blocking my browser was true of the browser but **not** the cause, and saying so beat accepting the neater story. ⬜ Not done: prod's captcha switch (release day, never before) · a Sam copy pass on the refusal sentence · a privacy policy. **⭐⭐ AND THE RELEASE FOUND SOMETHING BROKEN FOR MONTHS.** Released `main` → `prod` (PR #49 `cf0cb8e`, 61 commits, both migrations, tracker 151→153, workflows green) — then Sam said **"i dont see the widget on both dev and prod"** and asked me to check the tree, suspecting the interrupted session. **The tree was perfect**; ⚠ my own first check was not (`grep -c auth-turnstile` counts the inlined CSS rule too, so it proved nothing). The bundle said `sitekey: r` with **no `0x4AAAAAAE…` literal in either environment's JavaScript**. **⭐ CAUSE: `wrangler.jsonc` `vars` are RUNTIME bindings while `NEXT_PUBLIC_*` is a BUILD-TIME substitution**, and neither workflow passed any env to its build step (nor `--env prod`, which only the deploy carries) — so the server rendered the widget's container and hydration removed it. **⚠ THE HALF THAT WAS NOT ABOUT TURNSTILE:** the deployed `/reset-password` chunk called `createBrowserClient(n.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL, …)` **unreplaced**, so it and **`/welcome` (invite acceptance) had NEVER worked on either Worker** — every test of those flows ran on localhost, where `.env.local` is present at build time, which is exactly why months of sessions missed it. Fixed `ac822dc` (`env:` on both build steps, duplication flagged as debt both sides), verified on the dev Worker by reading the rebuilt bundle and by Sam seeing the widget, re-released (PR #50 `fccc9db`) with prod correctly carrying **prod** values — the question I had flagged as unverifiable. **⭐ PROD LIVE:** switch flipped, allowlist set; the back-door probe that returned `invalid_credentials` an hour earlier now returns **`captcha_failed`**; prod's logbook holds its first three rows — `LOGIN_OK`, `LOGIN_FAIL`, `LOGIN_OK` 24 s later, the typo retry succeeding on prod with Supabase verifying. ⚠ **Lesson: a green merge is not evidence of a green deploy (08-06), and a green deploy is not evidence of a working page (today)** — and it surfaced only because Sam pushed back on a release I had called green. New CLAUDE.md Known Workaround: any `NEXT_PUBLIC_*` goes in THREE places (`.env.local`, `wrangler.jsonc` vars, and the build step's `env:` in BOTH deploy workflows). ⏭ Next = slice ③ email-code login, or ④ attaching nclex.quademia.com. Prod current: `fccc9db`.](sessions/2026-08.md)
- [2026-08 — **2026-08-06 (evening) — slice 2c: the thresholds, and the countdown that told the truth — MERGED to `main`:** on-machine, branch `claude/work-session-bff2a4`, **4 commits, NO migration** — 2a had already built everything 2c needed (the `(email, occurred_at DESC)` index and the `*_BLOCKED` event types), which is what shipping the logbook first bought. vitest **919 → 939**, eslint clean, tsc at the known errors, production build green. **⚠ THE SESSION OPENED ON A DEPLOY THAT NEVER RAN** — Sam's *"the last merge seems to have not been deployed"* was right: the two most recent pushes to `main` both failed the dev-Worker deploy after exactly **15m 3s** with *"job was not acquired by Runner of type hosted"*, caused by a **GitHub Actions major outage**, not our code. Proven not assumed (`/forgot-password` 404 on the dev Worker while `/login` was 200 → it was still serving the pre-slice-2 build). ⭐ **A green merge to `main` is not evidence of a green dev deploy, and nothing tells you when they diverge.** Rules built: login **5/10min + 10/24h**, reset **3/60min**, per email; reset has no long rule ON PURPOSE (a reset link gains an attacker nothing however long he waits). **⭐ SAM'S QUESTION RESHAPED THE SLICE** — *"was gamma doing email alone? I think plus device too"* — it was, and reading gamma's SQL rather than our summary showed **its `fp_hash` axis carries NO email filter**, so the axes catch opposite attacks: email = many attempts on ONE address, device = ONE machine spraying MANY. Dropping the fingerprint removes the only rule watching that door; Turnstile is a better substitute (it binds the direct endpoint too) **but only once 2d ships**. Three departures from gamma: **fails OPEN** (a broken query must not become "nobody can sign in"); the countdown reads the **Nth-newest** row, not `MIN()` — gamma's under-reports past the threshold and **a countdown that lies is worse than none**; no device axis. **⭐ SAM CAUGHT THE UNTESTED BRANCH** (*"we didn't test the 24 hour gate"*) — tested by seeding 9 failures 2 h apart so the short rule couldn't fire; ⓘ **no bug found**, but it exposed two silently-failing assumptions, now pinned and mutation-checked. **⭐ THEN HE WALKED THE TIMELINE AND FOUND THE DESIGN HOLE:** on the 10th failure both rules trip, the longer wins, and the block is **23½ h anchored to her FIRST failure** — so *"come back in 24 hours"* doesn't state the wrong duration, **it answers the wrong question**. The long block now offers `set a new password instead` (separate counter, works immediately); the short block deliberately does not. Flagged not fixed: a successful login doesn't clear the counter, and the 24 h rule can catch a real student. ⬜ **NEXT = 2d Turnstile** — ⚠ sequencing trap (flip Supabase's captcha switch AFTER our own verification, or every auth call breaks), and **blocked on Sam creating the Cloudflare keys**.](sessions/2026-08.md)
- [2026-08 — **2026-08-06 (later still) — the identity arc writes its first code: slices 2a + 2b — MERGED to `main`:** on-machine, branch `claude/session-adb488`, **5 commits**, ONE migration (`20260904120000_auth_events.sql`, **dev only** — prod on the next release); vitest **887 → 919**, eslint clean, tsc at the known errors, production build green. **The top auth gap closes** — before today a student who forgot her password was locked out permanently. **2a** = `nclex_auth_events`, shipped BEFORE the flow it serves because logs cannot be captured retroactively; ⭐ append-only is **enforced, not remembered** (one SELECT policy, no insert/update/delete policy for anybody, writes over the top via service role — required anyway, since a FAILED login has no session for a policy to match). **2b** = `/forgot-password` + `/reset-password` + the login link, verified end to end. **⭐ SAM'S CATCH RESHAPED THE DESIGN:** *"what the student sees on the form and what admin will see later can be different"* — the anti-enumeration rule constrains what the PAGE may say, not what we may KNOW, so `LOGIN_FAIL` now records `wrong_password` vs `no_such_account` while the student still sees one identical message. **⭐ THE IP DECISION FLIPPED ON HIS PUSH** — the "store none" advice had conflated storing with blocking; blocking is the dangerous half (Ghanaian carrier NAT could lock out a whole network), logging is the only axis that sees a spray → full IP, logged, never enforced on. **⚠ `/reset-password` TOOK THREE ATTEMPTS, THE FIRST TWO BUILT ON STORIES ABOUT THE LIBRARY RATHER THAN ITS SOURCE** — `?code=` and `#access_token=` need OPPOSITE handling and `detectSessionInUrl` cannot be configured off; two wrong diagnoses came from tests that never ran, because a fragment-only navigation doesn't reload the page. Both written into CLAUDE.md → Known Workarounds, since slice 3 meets the same trap. Closed with a Sam-led **copy pass** — his "Invalid link" was talked down to **"This link no longer works"** (the link WAS valid; "invalid" reads as an accusation, or as though the email had been phishing), and his "Try your spam folder" replaced an imperative that sent people past the inbox they'd already checked. ✅ Sam fixed dev's stale Site URL. ⭐ **NEXT = 2c**, the per-email thresholds.](sessions/2026-08.md)
- [2026-08 — **2026-08-06 (later) — the four templates became two, and Sam remembered a feature we never wrote down — docs only, MERGED to `main`:** on-machine, branch `claude/work-session-10dbe8`, **3 commits all documentation**; no app code, no migration. Build-order items **① and ② both CLOSED** — auth templates live on dev + prod, DKIM + DMARC live and DNS-verified. **⭐ Sam's decision deleted work rather than adding it:** the plan said brand FOUR templates with invite worded "neutrally"; his objection — *an invite is never just an invite, it always arrives attached to a programme or bank access* — showed **the neutral wording was not a solution to the shared-template problem, it was the problem restated**. Four became two (reset-password + confirm-signup); invite goes custom via `generateLink({type:'invite'})`; change-email dropped on evidence (nothing calls `updateUser({email})`). **⭐ That exposed a contradiction nobody had noticed:** `transactional-email.md` called Invite the pre-`/welcome` step while its own catalog had `enrolment.confirmed`/`enrolment.tutor_added` doing the job richly — as written an invited student got TWO emails. Fixed, with ⚠ **do not disable the Supabase invite before the replacement ships** (it is the only email an invitee gets today). New **`docs/email/`** folder — an asset, not a plan — carrying **repo is the source, a dashboard is a copy**. **⭐ DKIM verified by lookup, not by report:** 2048-bit provable from the key's own header; DMARC `p=none`; root SPF still Google-only with Resend's on `send.` — **two SPF records that never needed merging because they are on different names**. ⚠ DNS cannot prove DKIM is ON — the "Start authentication" click has no DNS footprint. New **Launch gates** section: email confirmation stays OFF (Sam's reason right; blast radius measured — `signUp()` is in exactly ONE file) but must flip at launch, or a mistyped address becomes an unrecoverable account. **Slice 2 scoped, not built** — incl. gamma's `EMAIL_SENT`/`EMAIL_FAILED` catch (log the outcome of the send, not the request) and ⚠ Turnstile's sequencing trap. **⭐ Sam remembered a feature the doc had never heard of** — concurrent device/session limits — researched gamma + Supabase: **only ONE of Supabase's three session settings addresses sharing**, and it is exactly 1 not N, enforced at token refresh; single-session rejected (it logs a student out of her laptop when she opens her phone); framed honestly as **a revenue problem in a security costume**; **capture first** — 2a's device labels make sharing visible before any number is chosen, so no build-order slot assigned. ⬜ Open before 2a: IP column (none recommended) · retention window (90d proposed) · future event types in the constraint now.](sessions/2026-08.md)

- [2026-08 — **2026-08-03 (later) — two cloud branches come home, and a "defect" that was never one — MERGED to `main`:** **3 commits** (one merge, two docs), **no migration**, no schema change, **no app code written this session**; vitest **887/887**; tsc at the known errors; production build green; the 1 eslint warning **attributed pre-existing** by checking the pre-merge blob, not assumed. **⭐ THE SESSION EXISTED BECAUSE `origin` HAD BRANCHES ON IT** — Sam: *"first check remote, there are new braches."* Two, from 2026-08-01 **cloud** sessions that never reached this machine. ⚠ **The "origin carries only main + prod" rule is about SESSION branches on this machine, not a description of the remote** — a cloud session pushes, and its work is invisible until someone fetches. One had already absorbed the other, so there was **one** branch to merge, not two. **What came home:** `db/seed/pitch/` — the demo a prospective tutor logs into (9 seed files, 4 generators, README, ~10.5k lines): two logins, 18 seeded students, an 8-week flagship, a crash course, the four entry paths, maternity packaged four ways, and the free weekly Q&A as a **52-week taught year**. All **dev-only** (`migrate-prod.yml` reads `db/migrations/` alone). **⭐ THE ONE PIECE OF REAL CODE IS A BUG SAM DIAGNOSED HIMSELF** — Postgres **ORs** a table's SELECT policies, so a user who is both tutor and student passed `nclex_programmes`' ownership branch and saw every programme they teach in their STUDENT list; the action leaned on RLS to narrow it. **RLS is a security boundary, not a view filter.** **⭐ THE DEMO RESET WAS MISSING THE TWO FILES WORTH SHOWING** — the README's restore order stopped at `07`, but maternity is `08` and the year is `09`, so resetting between pitches silently rebuilt the demo **without either**. ⚠ Re-runnability **verified rather than trusted** (every INSERT carries `ON CONFLICT` — **35/35** and **8/8**; `09` rebases on `CURRENT_DATE`); "two of them are generated" → **four**. **⭐ SAM KILLED A "DEFECT" I HAD RELAYED TWICE** — *"i dont think there is a problem, you have to check well."* The 2026-08-01 log called empty cohort checklists **"the most likely thing to bite Sam's first real programme on prod"**; I repeated it, then verified and found the trigger genuinely absent, `createCohortAction` not seeding, and the student query reading **solely** through the checklist — all true, conclusion still wrong. ⚠ **I had not asked WHY it was absent:** a migration drops it **deliberately**, a model change **settled with Sam 2026-06-01** — the checklist became an **OVERRIDE on the live template**, and **a new cohort with zero rows is the design**. ⚠ Absent on **prod too** — the check that would have killed it fastest. ⓘ Sam's model is right for the **tutor's** view, not the student's. **⚠ TWO OF MY CLAIMS RETRACTED:** *"nothing would warn you"* (there is an **"N unconfigured → Include all"** prompt) and the **"ghost rows"**, which were **my own query's fault** — joined through blocks when an activity may sit directly under a unit; His was the strip being crushed between scenario and content: a scroll container's automatic minimum height is **0**, so the 52px strip and the body absorbed the entire shortfall while the scenario (`overflow: visible`) could not shrink a pixel — **the only control reaching the other charts was sacrificed first**. - [2026-07 — **2026-07-30 (later still — the review SCORING STRIP — RELEASED TO PROD):** **10 commits, one migration** (`20260903120000_item_response_stats.sql`), dev-applied; vitest **784 → 855**; tsc at the known errors. **On prod** (PR #46 + a follow-up release). Doc: `docs/product-plan/scoring-strip.md`. Built from Sam's Claude Design prototype. **⭐ THE STRIP'S REAL JOB WAS NOT THE NEW FIGURES** — the rationale header read `Rationale · wrong` off `is_correct`, which is **full-credit-only by design**, so a student scoring 2 of 3 was told "wrong" with "2 / 3" beside it. **199 of 715 dev answers (28%) are partial**, so one review screen in four was doing this. Fixed first and alone, and reached the tutor-library embeds free. **Sam proposed making `is_correct` three-state; pushed back** — score + marks already determine the answer, so a third column restates two others and can drift (the difficulty word/number shape that cost 10d), and **57 TS + 24 SQL files** read that flag wanting it strict. His underlying rule — three states *everywhere* — adopted as a shared function. **⭐ SAM KILLED THE NAME "COHORT"**, the prototype's word, carried by me unquestioned: already taken (~219 files), and it points at the **wrong set** — a tutor would read "my class" when this is every student ever. **The proof: the word is accurate for the one view where we don't use it and inaccurate for the one where we did.** Now **item statistics**. **⭐ THREE ARCHITECTURE QUESTIONS FROM SAM, ALL OF WHICH CHANGED THE ANSWER:** derived or stored (**stored** — five surfaces would each re-implement three rules, one of them a *safety* rule, since below the threshold a percentage is the reader's own result handed back); why a table not columns on the question (**checked, not assumed** — `nclex_bank_items` is readable by every signed-in user, an AFTER UPDATE audit trigger would fill the "who edited this" log with machine writes, and three BEFORE UPDATE triggers fire on any update); and extend the recalibration Action (**no** — `recalibrate.yml`'s own header states the rule, and ⚠ my "it rides free on a pass we already make" was **retracted**, since that job reads BANK only, deliberately). **⭐ SAM ADDED TUTOR QUESTIONS AND CORRECTED ME** — I called the figure structurally bank-only because a tutor never reaches 30 students, **conflating the data with the display gate**. Now **two audiences, two gates**: students get a percentage at n≥30, tutors get their own questions with **no threshold**, because *a percentage needs a large n and a fraction does not*. **The switch gates the DISPLAY as well as the job** — stopping the job alone would *freeze* wrong numbers on screen. The threshold deliberately did **not** become an admin knob. **THE GRID GAINED A SIXTH FILL** so it could stop contradicting the strip; **amber moved from Skipped to Partial** (Sam), Skipped becoming a dashed neutral because it is not a score but the *absence* of an answer. ⚠ **The tutorial teaches the colour key in words** ("amber skipped"), so that step became **wrong, not dated**, on a public walkthrough. ⚠ `deriveCellFill` — deciding every cell's colour — had **no tests at all**; `gridCounts` was **dead code**, deleted. **⭐ SAM REWORDED ALL SEVEN SCORING RULES** (the first time the product tells a student how marks were worked out — they had existed only as code comments), and **his Matrix-multi-response is better than mine**: literally the SATA sentence with `Per row — ` in front, so the copy **is** the relationship rather than describing it, 19 chars shorter. ⚠ The copy carries a **machine-checkable claim** run against the real scorer. ⚠ **⚠ FOUR DEFECTS, ALL FROM READING THE RENDERED PAGE**, none from tsc/tests/lint: the tooltip **dropped 41% of students** (single-mark "did not" read as the zero share when it means *not full marks*); it **totalled 101%**; it was **as wide as the screen** (a native `title` is one unbroken line, so the line breaks *are* the layout — 185 chars → 39); and my draft said **"not a verdict on you"** when `verdict` names the chip two inches away. **VERIFIED:** the SQL/TS agreement test **reads the migration off disk and evaluates its FILTER conditions** against `verdictFor`, proven to bite; refresh reconciled **600/600** against an independent recomputation; RPC proven **under rollback** (threshold, ownership, switch); both bodies **md5-identical to the file**, which they had **not** been. ⚠ **The figure cannot be seen on dev naturally** — most-answered question has **3** answers against a threshold of 30; staged data left in place on Sam's call, self-clearing at the next cron run. **⭐ THEN SLICE 3b — the colour key BECAME the filter**, closing a gap slice 3 opened (once partial had its own fill, no filter could reach it). Sam's cut was sharper than either of mine: **the rail is progress, the key is outcome**, so "Wrong" simply LEAVES the rail rather than moving — ⚠ and his suggested replacement "Answered" was checked against the data and dropped, since that fill occurs **zero** times in review. **"Dropped marks" = wrong + partial**, the union single-select cannot express. ⚠ The key was `aria-hidden` decoration and is now real buttons with explicit spoken names. ⚠ Two stale strings named filters that no longer exist. ⬜ Not built: skipped still reads WRONG in the strip · tap-to-reveal on phones · multi-select · the report/curator surfaces. ⚠ **The runner is badly broken at phone widths** (pre-existing, own session).](sessions/2026-07.md)
- [2026-07 — **2026-07-30 (later — marking, built as TWO features: flag + bookmark — MERGED to `main`):** **6 commits, one migration** (`20260902120000_attempt_item_flag.sql`), dev-applied; vitest **739 → 784**; tsc at the known errors. **On prod (released `e906ae5`).** Doc: `docs/product-plan/flag-and-bookmark.md`, written first and built after. **⭐ SAM'S QUESTION — "do we have a documented plan on the mark?" — IS WHAT UNLOCKED IT.** The honest answer was that the plumbing was specified and the experience was not (`bank-consumption-runner.html` §14 is four sentences tagged *skeleton*), and that gap mattered because **"mark" was two features sharing one icon** — which, not a missing writer, is why it sat half-built since 2026-05. Sam named them: **flag** = "come back before I submit"; **bookmark** = "save this to study again". Then asked whether one thing could do both. **It cannot, and the proof is one line:** `nclex_question_marks`'s unique index is `(student, kind, target)` with **no `attempt_id`** — the same question flagged in two sittings is refused, and unflagging in one deletes the row an older report needs. It is a *bookmark* table. The flag went on `nclex_attempt_items`, already one row per (attempt, question). **Separating was LESS work than merging.** **BOOKMARK FIRST** (no migration, no RPC) because it closed a Builder filter that had **never once matched a question**; verified rather than assumed that the pool helper already reads bookmarks correctly, so **the read side needed no SQL change at all**. **⭐ SAM NARROWED THE SCOPE THREE TIMES, CORRECTLY EACH TIME:** ① no bookmark in CAT or readiness, live *or* review — and for a better reason than exam authenticity: reserved stock is excluded from `_nclex_eligible_unit_pool` and the bookmark filter is a **CTE inside that helper**, so a bookmarked CAT question **can never be served back**; ⚠ writing it found a **third** exclusion the doc missed — **tutor quizzes**, since the pool is bank-only ② bookmarks are **questions only**, and he predicted the mechanic before I checked: bookmarking one case child pulls the whole case, because a case is one selection unit — already the built behaviour, identical to every other filter, and arguably *correct* here since you need the scenario to answer the child ③ a question already bookmarked "must not be bookmarkable again" — right, but **not a rule to enforce, a LOAD to perform**: read existing bookmarks at sitting start or the control renders "off" and the tap hits the unique index. **⭐ THAT EXPOSED THE TRAP THAT DEFINES THE ARC** — the grid's set is keyed by `attempt_item_id`, so it was built for the FLAG all along; the two features need **two different keys** (flag = per-sitting, starts empty; bookmark = per-student, arrives pre-set), both are `Set<string>`, and **passing one where the other belongs compiles cleanly**. Guarded by naming and by a test asserting the predicates **disagree** where they should. **⭐ THEN SAM KILLED THE FLAG CARD IN THE REPORT AND CAUGHT AN OVER-CLAIM OF MINE:** *"what if I flagged questions then unflagged them all before submitting?"* — the report shows nothing, correct, but it exposes that a flag is a weak signal there (flagged-but-tidied vs unflagged-but-still-lost) **and** that *"you flagged 6 during this sitting"* is unsupportable, since unflagging **deletes** the state and the report only ever sees the position at submit. I had written that sentence in the doc, a comment and two commit messages. Replaced by **still-bookmarked, read LIVE at view time** — deliberate, durable, and the only signal there that is **actionable**; ranked **first** in the fix list because the student's own intent outranks our inference. ⚠ The wording rule is now **a test** (bookmarks have no attempt scoping, so "still bookmarked", never "you bookmarked these here"). Plus an inline star toggle, making the report a second home for the pruning loop; and one rule bent — deep links force pool=UNSEEN, but a bookmark says the opposite in as many words, so `pool=marked` is honoured, narrowly. **⚠ SLICE 5 WAS BIGGER THAN PLANNED, AND I CAUSED IT** — the sandbox **hid** both controls, so the coach anchors I added pointed at elements that never mounted; fixed by making the sandbox render them for real and skip only the write. CD's one "Mark for review" step became **two**; the flag step is **gated**, the bookmark step deliberately is **not** (a bookmark persists, and the sandbox promises nothing is recorded). **⚠ TWO SILENT BREAKAGES, BOTH MINE, BOTH CAUGHT:** `COACH_SECTIONS` holds **raw indices** into `COACH_STEPS`, so two inserted steps misaligned every section below them with **tsc clean and all tests green** (now pinned to step titles, and **proven to bite** — it caught two wrong expectations on its first run); and the coach's gate check treated *anything not `calc`* as the submit gate, so a `flag` gate would have silently required **submitting**. **RPC PROVEN 12 WAYS** under rollback — ⚠ the CAT check first passed for the **wrong reason** (dev has zero in-progress CATs, so the status guard fired first), re-proved by borrowing a finished CAT into `IN_PROGRESS` and restoring it; ⚠ the deployed body and the file had **diverged** by two dropped comments, caught **only by md5**. **VOCABULARY SWEEP — one string was WRONG, not dated:** the public bank-access demo called its bookmark pool *"Flagged for review mid-quiz"*, teaching the exact conflation, on the first page a prospective student reads. ⚠ **THE DEV SERVER KEPT DYING AND vitest IS WHY** — in a worktree its `.vite` cache creates a near-empty `node_modules/` that makes Turbopack infer the wrong root; `next dev` says Ready then dies on first compile. Start the server BEFORE running tests. ⚠ **Verification is uneven:** Sam tested slices 1–3 live; **slice 4 (report) and slice 5's coach copy have NOT been clicked**. ⚠ The "2 known tsc errors" is now **one file** — `validator.ts` was a stale `.next` artefact. ⬜ Not built: "My bookmarks" · a case-bank bookmark filter · the topbar overflow menu · case-level bookmarking · mark history.](sessions/2026-07.md)
- [2026-07 — **2026-07-30 (History becomes a directory, and practice gets a Session Report — RELEASED TO PROD):** **9 commits, one migration** (`20260901120000_discard_attempt.sql`), dev-applied; vitest **648 → 739**; tsc at the 2 known errors; production build run before each merge. Doc: `docs/product-plan/session-report.md`. **⭐ SAM RESHAPED IT WITH ONE OBJECTION.** The brief was "the History page is a placeholder, build it for real"; I proposed putting practice detail *inside* History, and Sam's reply — *"then we would have to make another page specially for practice quizzes… the history page becomes just like a log of your attempt table"* — is the right shape.
