proven under rollback **7/7** incl. another student's sitting and a fabricated id refused with the **same message** so it cannot probe for existence; migration **md5-identical** to the deployed body. **THE REPORT, THREE SLICES** from CD's "1c" across three review rounds — the first two kept a marked card that cannot be built, put the no-banding line in a footnote, and had no phone design; `pointsDetail()` was already the found/missed/wrong-picked splitter, so the priciest-looking card was a reuse, **verified reconciling live: 19 found − 3 wrong = 16 = the stored score**. **⭐ SEVEN FINDINGS, ALL FROM THE RENDERED PAGE OR THE DATA, none from tests/types/lint:** ① the first report **contradicted itself** ("9 Q" vs "2 of 6") — `actual_question_count` is wrong on **7 of 69** attempts; fixed in the report *and* History ② **`answer_changes_json` cannot be counted by length** (append-only, includes the first answer, and *building* a multi-slot answer appends one entry per slot — a 4-row matrix logs four entries and zero changes of mind); its saving grace is storing the answer **before and after**, which makes *"1 of those moved away from the right answer"* derivable ③ **139 of 1,853 stems (7.5%) are Tiptap JSON** — one row in thirteen would print `{"type":"doc",…}` ④ **`nursing_subject` is in ZERO stored payloads**, so a subject-only search could never match ⑤ the confirm dialog **inherited `white-space: nowrap`** from its table cell (fixed leaves the FLOW, not the CASCADE) — ⚠ my first fix addressed a real line-height gap but **not what was on screen**, because my harness rendered the dialog standalone ⑥ the table was **clipped at 1066px** (872px in a 791px `overflow:hidden` card), a priority inversion that squeezed **Session to 92px** ⑦ the fix list said "in that order" then numbered a **75% subject above a 0% category**. **⭐ THEN SAM MADE IT CONSISTENT:** the practice report was the only one you had to *discover*, so every finished row now offers the same pair, **Report** and **Review**. ⚠ **Packs are asymmetric** (answers expire at 21 days) — and **a defect in my own first cut, found in the data**: I defaulted an unknown window to OPEN, but the runner treats a *missing* credit row as expired and only **2** pack attempts have one, so that default would have offered Review on nearly every pack row and bounced every one; now fails closed, proven against the runner. **⭐ HE ALSO CAUGHT A CARD I DROPPED** — the rail's "On this page" jump list, an omission not a decision (I recorded CD's *phone* note for it and never built the desktop version). **⚠ MARKING IS HALF-BUILT AND THIS SESSION PINNED IT DOWN:** the Builder offers a Marked pool chip, the runner has a ⚑ Mark button, the grid has a Marked filter, the table exists and SQL reads it — **and nothing writes to it** (the button is `disabled`, tooltip "not available yet"), so the Marked pool has never matched a question. CD's three marked-based elements are all deliberately absent. **Sam: build marking later, properly** — only the write path is missing. **ALSO:** the dashboard rail offered **two bounce links** (abandoned → a report that redirects, mine; in-progress pack → the pack report, pre-existing) because it routed by kind before status. ⚠ Not built: marking · "re-quiz what you got wrong" (the Builder *has* an INCORRECT chip but its prefill deliberately forces UNSEEN) · per-question deep links · History sorting/date filters. Renaming History was **reviewed and dropped** — "test history" is the market convention; nav reads "All history". ⭐ **RELEASED TO PROD the same session** (PR #44, `b8c7e82`) together with the case-bank arc — 11 commits, 4 migrations, none previously on prod; both workflows green, tracker 145 → 149; pre-release check run as a set comparison, zero orphan rows. ⚠ Nobody has clicked either arc on the prod deployment. ⏭ Next = **build marking**.](sessions/2026-07.md)
- [2026-07 — **2026-07-29/30 (the student Case Study bank — MERGED to `main`):** built `/student/bank/cases` from scratch (Sam: build it properly, let CD upgrade later) — browse every published case study, sit **1 or 2** at a time in the **existing runner**. **6 commits, 3 migrations** (`20260829120000`, `20260830120000`, `20260831120000`), dev-applied; vitest **611 → 648**; tsc at the 2 known errors. **⚠ FIRST, A CORRECTION TO EVERY RECENT ENTRY:** they say "prod still `1a6717d`" — false since 2026-07-29's release PR #43 (`e636665`); prod carries `main`'s tip and the whole CAT arc is live. Never trust a pinned SHA in a log. **⭐ THE UNLOCK RULE IS SAM'S AND IT'S THE BEST IDEA IN THE FEATURE:** reserved cases (CAT pool / readiness pack) render locked, but once a student has actually MET one in a CAT or pack it **unlocks for them** — protection expires when it stops protecting anything. Most products lock forever (you can never redrill the case you just struggled with in a paid pack) or don't lock (leak the exam); this is a third answer, and it turns packs and CAT into a **source** of practice material. "Seen" = the case was in one of their attempts AND (that attempt finished **OR** they submitted an answer to one of its questions) — **both halves load-bearing**, since a pack snapshots every question the moment the attempt row exists while an abandoned CAT genuinely showed what it served; ⚠ confirmed live that merely *visiting* a question writes a DRAFT answer row, so "an answer row exists" would have been wrong, and proven to discriminate **within one in-progress attempt**. **⭐ SAM'S CORRECTION MID-DESIGN — the student must never learn WHY a case is locked** ("thats telling them our system"), which has a technical consequence beyond copy: **the reason must not be in the payload**, or it's readable in the response while merely hidden in the UI. So the RPC returns a bare `locked`, and a locked case ships **no scenario text at all** (that's exam content). ⚠ **Verified to BITE:** against the live 100KB response a locked case's scenario is absent **while an unlocked case's is present** — a guard only seen passing is not a guard. (An earlier check of mine cried leak: false positive from my own loose regex hitting "**Cat**heter" and "compli**cat**ions".) **ARCHITECTURE — own SQL, nothing shared touched:** one eligibility helper read by **both** the list and launch RPCs so they cannot disagree; deliberately **NOT** `_nclex_eligible_unit_pool`, because teaching that the unlock rule would leak reserved stock into the practice builder — 10b3's whole guarantee. Helper granted to **nobody**. Launch RPC **14/14 under rollback**. **ROW CONTENT IS NOT THE PROTOTYPE'S** — dropped "6 questions" (identical on all 93 rows), the difficulty word (the average case spans **1.90 logits**, re-introducing the exact drift 10d had just removed) and raw `tags` (⚠ one value is literally **`readiness`**, which would have named the mechanism); replaced by the **dominant** subject+body system, since listing every distinct one dragged "Leadership and Management" onto a peritoneal-dialysis case and ran to **140 chars**, longer than its own title → 42. ⚠ `scenario_summary` holds **two formats in one column** (71 Tiptap / 22 plain). **⭐ A REAL BUG ONLY CLICKING FOUND:** the third-case refusal showed no toast — `setError` inside a `setSelected` updater, and returning state unchanged makes React bail out of the re-render; **tsc clean, all tests green**, same class as the 1,000-row cap. **⭐ THEN SAM ASKED HOW THE PAGE IS GUARDED, WHICH FOUND TWO HOLES:** ① the list RPC checked only *signed in*, not *entitled* — it returns **content**, unlike its auth-only siblings which return counts; ② `is_builder_visible` was described in the design and then **not built** (mine) — and ⚠ it cannot be a plain `AND`, because reserving **forces that flag off**, so the naive fix would re-lock all 13 earned cases; rule is `reserved OR is_builder_visible`. Both **proven to bite** under rollback. **⭐ THEN SAM FOUND THE REVIEW DEFECT AND REDESIGNED AROUND IT:** Review opened the whole originating sitting — a **100-question CAT landing on question 100**, case at Q10–15, **7 of 16** attempted cases. Sam's fix: **"Already attempted" means SAT HERE**, so Review can only reach this page's own 1–2 case runs; then *"when a student has reattempted several times we must see the other result"* — exposing that only the **latest** sitting was kept, discarding the progression that is the point of reattempting; then show the exam sitting too, as the first attempt, with its score. All three converge on **the expanded row becoming the case's own history**, each entry linking to its own home (readiness → pack report, CAT → result page) so **no entry can open a 100-question runner**; plus `answered`/`served`, because a bare percentage conflated "did badly" with "didn't finish" (a real row: 50% that was 75% on the four of six answered; another with 0 of 6 rendering identically to a genuine zero) → **"Not answered"**, grey not red. **ALSO:** top pane widened with the run rail beneath it; the runner's **exit resolver** learned this surface (a case run is `CUSTOM_BUILT` so it exited to the practice builder — the same trap the file already documented for CAT; `filters_json` now **required** on its input type); nav relabelled **NGN Case Studies** + **Adaptive Testing (CAT)**; **Journey Tracker placeholders removed** (route, nav, dashboard door — zero `journey_*` tables ever existed; design + CLAUDE.md rule #1 exception untouched), its door replaced by NGN Case Studies with a real count verified 37 = 37, and ⭐ **every dashboard door now goes somewhere**, with a test asserting none can be dead. **VERIFIED:** a real 12-question sitting played end to end (Case 1 of 2 → Case 2 of 2); **13 reserved cases takeable for a student who met them, zero takeable without**; mobile clean at 375px; console clean; all four function bodies **md5-identical to their files**. ⚠ A completed test sitting left on dev so "Already attempted" is populated. ⚠ **THE OPEN QUESTION IS CONTENT SUPPLY** — 59 of 93 dev cases are CAT-reserved and 10 are in packs, and CAT's own target is 60, so the two compete for the same shelf and the page gets *emptier* as CAT fills; author more cases, don't reallocate (prod: 7 published-complete, none reserved). ⬜ Not built: deep-link Review to the case's first question (still lands on the run's last — 12 questions now, not 100) · a coverage signal · a case-scoped review surface. Doc: `docs/product-plan/case-bank.md`. ⏭ **Next = the student History page.**](sessions/2026-07.md)
- [2026-07 — **2026-07-29 (later still — Slice 10d + the CAT-reservation guard — **RELEASED TO PROD** PR #43, `e636665`):** **3 code commits + docs, 3 migrations** (`20260826120000`, `20260827120000`, `20260828120000`), dev-applied; vitest **606 → 611**; tsc at the 2 known errors. **⭐ ALSO CLOSED: the long-standing "editor tick is a second reservation door" ⚠ — it is a FOURTH-door problem.** Reserving carries three obligations beyond setting `cat_pool` (leave the practice builder · stop being a free sample · not also be sold in a readiness pack) and all three lived in **one** caller, the admin drawer's action; the two editor ticks write the flag bare and **the drawer's own action never re-checks pack membership for cases**, only `item` targets. ⚠ **I reported that third door wrong and corrected it before committing** — claimed a one-click leak on the sanctioned surface (10 published cases with pack-member children); **untrue, the drawer's UI greys them** (`queries.ts` rolls child pack membership into the wrapper's `readinessTagged`). I found the gap in the action and assumed the UI shared it without looking. What IS missing: the **server-side** re-check the item path has (whose own comment says *don't trust what the drawer displayed*), so a stale tab or concurrent pack edit goes through; plus the UI rollup counts only **published** children. Both the migration header and a code comment had the wrong version and now state it correctly **including that it was overstated**. **FIX = a `BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE` trigger** on items + cases forcing both flags false and refusing a pack member by name — a trigger not a 4th TS copy because four paths set the flag, three skip part of the contract, and the DB is the only layer all four cross. **Forcing flags but REJECTING on pack membership** is the judgement: ticking "Reserve for CAT" states an intention the flags follow from, so making them agree completes it; pack membership is two intentions in conflict. ⚠ `is_free_sample` forced on **cases** too, which the drawer never did. ⚠ **THE TRIGGER NAME IS LOAD-BEARING** — Postgres fires same-event row triggers **alphabetically** and `..._inherit_trg` copies a child's flag from its parent, so the guard must sort AFTER it; `..._seal_trg` is named for that alone and a tidier earlier-sorting name silently disables it on INSERT. **Proven on real rows in a rolled-back transaction, 5/5**, incl. a case refused **transitively via the cascade** with the error naming the offending CHILD; 2,833 reserved rows verified untouched after. **⚠ FIRST, A CORRECTION TO EVERY RECENT LOG:** they all say "prod still `1a6717d`" — false since the CAT arc released. `origin/prod` is `885ecc1` carrying `main`'s tip, so 10a/10b/10c are **live on prod**, recalibration job included. **THE SLICE:** a question's difficulty exists twice — the curator's word and the measured number — and §5.2 forbids recalibration rewriting the word, so it goes stale on purpose. `displayBand()` shipped in 10a to close that drift and **had never once fired**, because the attempt snapshot froze the *word* and the number never reached the pill. 10d changes what is frozen. **Bodies were never retyped** — no local SQL client exists, so each live definition was read with `pg_get_functiondef()`, replaced **under an assertion on the occurrence count**, executed, and dumped back through a temporary RPC called from Node; both migration files **byte-identical by md5** to what is deployed. **⭐ THREE FINDINGS, NONE CAUGHT BY ANY AUTOMATED CHECK.** ① "Six functions, one substitution each" was **ten lines** — a standalone question and a case child are frozen by *two separate builders* in two of the functions, and the tutor-quiz paths need their `SELECT` list widened; missing the case-child branch would have blanked the pill on **every case question, six per case**. ② **⭐ "The pill is the only reader — no SQL, no report, no analytics" was wrong; there were three.** The **readiness-pack report** froze the word into every report row and fed it to a breakdown table, the per-question detail, and a filter *and* grouping — the surface students pay for; and the **runner tutorial's** in-code fixtures carried it, so every pill in the public walkthrough would have gone blank. **tsc was clean and all 607 tests passed** — optional-field runtime shapes, so both would have shipped and been found by a human opening the page (same class as last session's 1,000-row cap). ③ **⭐ THE DRIFT RELOCATES TO THE FILTER — Sam's "step 2", which he thought was already done and wasn't.** The practice builder's difficulty axis is *chosen and counted in the database* on `bi.difficulty`, so a student picks by the word and is then shown a band derived from the number — **more** visible than the bug it replaced, since before part 1 both ends read the stale word and agreed with each other while both were wrong. Closed with a SQL `nclex_difficulty_band()` driving the filter predicate **and** the per-band counts in both branches of `_nclex_eligible_unit_pool` + `nclex_filter_breakdown`; count and attempt creation follow through the one gateway (the 10b3 property). ⚠ **Cost named not hidden — the cut-offs now exist in two languages.** A generated column (relocates the duplication, adds a column part 1 avoided) and TS-side numeric ranges (cleanest for the filter, but the live counts then need five ranges grouped by hand) both rejected; the two copies are instead **bound by a test that reads the cut-offs out of the migration file**, asymmetric operators included, and **verified to bite** — moving one to 1.6 fails it. A guard only ever seen passing is not a guard. **VERIFIED LIVE:** tutorial pill Medium/Easy/Hard from 0.0/−1.0/1.0; readiness difficulty axis **20/55/25 reconciling exactly** against SQL; builder five bands **158/152/250/188/191**, identical to calling the pool helper; ticking Hard gives **268 = 148 standalone + 120 children of the 20 cases holding a Hard child** (a case is all-or-nothing, so axis count ≠ match count — pre-existing). Console clean. **Dev backfill deliberately NOT a migration** (Sam's call — prod has **zero** attempt rows, so it would be a permanent no-op): 1,636 rows translated, **0 mistranslated**, every other classification key **byte-identical to a backup**. **Pre-existing 10a leftover fixed:** the report's per-question map had **two** hardcoded `['Easy','Medium','Hard']` lists silently dropping every Very easy / Very hard question since five bands shipped. ⚠ Not built: recalibrate-now button · history readout · misfit warnings. ⚠ The editor tick is still a second CAT-reservation door (latent, 0 rows). **⭐ THE MIGRATION-TRACKER ⚠ CARRIED BY THREE PRIOR ENTRIES IS RETIRED — it was aimed at the wrong database.** Measured both against the 144 files: **prod 142 rows, ZERO without a file** (the 2 missing are today's); dev 121 rows, **105 without a file**, 128 files untracked. `supabase db push` runs against **prod only** (`migrate-prod.yml` is the sole migration workflow, no dev equivalent), so **there is no release concern and never was**. ⚠ **The trap, recorded: version FORMAT is not the test** — prod has 34 wall-clock-looking versions and **34 files don't use the `120000` convention either**, matching one-for-one; pattern-matching the stamp gives a false positive, only a set comparison answers it (I made that mistake first). Dev's tracker isn't drifted, it's **fiction** — 16 rows of 144 agree — because dev is fed via MCP (`apply_migration` stamps a wall clock, raw `execute_sql` stamps nothing). **Left alone on Sam's call:** rewriting 105 rows would assert dev has all 144 applied, unverifiable row-by-row — a broken tracker fails loudly, a confidently-wrong one silently tells a future `db push` to skip a migration that never ran, and nothing reads dev's.](sessions/2026-07.md)
- [2026-07 — **2026-07-29 (later — Slice 10c: difficulty stops being an opinion — MERGED to `main`):** began as "check a few logics before we build 10c", and the audit paid for itself. **3 commits, 2 migrations** (`20260824120000`, `20260825120000`), dev-applied; vitest **565 → 606**; prod still `1a6717d`. **⭐ THE AUDIT FINDING — the CAT pool's membership test named the wrong column.** CAT selects on `difficulty_irt`, the number; the placeability CHECK, the reserve drawer, the audit lens and the reserve action all screened on `difficulty`, the curator's *word*. They're written together by the editor and **not** by bulk SQL — the 622-item gap-fill run set a band on every row and a number on none (13 seed files, zero mentions of the column), leaving **633 items with a word and no number**: **622 offerable in the reserve drawer** as CAT stock the engine could never serve, and **11 already reserved, counted in Coverage and dead**. Reserve a few hundred more and the targets would have read "met" while CAT served none of them. Sam's read — "just a bulk-seeding artefact" — was half right: the cause is benign, the state isn't, and nothing stopped it recurring; measuring the blast radius (633, not 11) settled it. Fixed by making the number **impossible to omit** (a trigger seeds it on any write) rather than tightening the four tests that missed it. A planned test proved unreachable — the taxonomy CHECK already closes the band to five values, so *band ⇒ number* is now total. Also: **CAT now honours a trend dataset's publish state**, which practice always has. ⚠ Still open — **the editor tick is a second reservation door** skipping three of the drawer's guards (latent, 0 rows). **⭐ SLICE 10c BUILT.** ⚠ **§17.6's stated reason was wrong**: "in-Postgres math does not do a joint fit" — it can; the real deciders are that **17.6% of answers carry partial credit** (SQL would flatten them to right/wrong, making every SATA/matrix/bow-tie read harder than it is), silent failure, and CPU. ⚠ **TypeScript, not the Python/`pyirt` the doc named** — there is **no Python on the machine** (same blind spot that shipped `validate.py` untested) and session branches never reach the remote, so a Python job could not have been run once before merging; an unrunnable estimator loses on *correctness*, the whole reason the job left the database. **⭐ Sam's question reshaped the maths** — "didn't we agree how many of five count as right?" §4.5 settled it (the fraction, never a threshold) *and* names the consistency as binding; it caught that the scoped "5-mark question = five mini-questions" framing weights big questions 5–13× heavier than the engine does — the exact drift §4.5 exists to prevent. Corrected to **fraction in, one question counts as one**, which made the build smaller: the person half **IS `estimateAbility` from `lib/cat`**, shared not copied, so drift is impossible rather than forbidden — and it anchors calibrated difficulty to the passing standard. `lib/cat/` needed **no edit**. **Sam corrected the folder too** — calibration covers the whole bank (§5.3.5), so `lib/calibration/`, not under `cat/`. A **failing test caught the mirror image**: the weight cancels in the item's own step, so a case child calibrates at FULL strength while counting HALF toward ability — exactly the §19.4 split, delivered by one number with no special case. ⚠ **The audit table already existed** since Slice 1 (§12.7.5) and §17.2's column list nearly produced a **second one**; adapted, adding `raw_empirical_value` + `job_trigger`/`job_triggered_by`. Writes go through **one RPC, one transaction**. Switch = the enrolment sweep's pattern (Sam's precedent), shipped ON because self-gating. **PROVEN:** recovers chosen difficulties from a simulated cohort (mean error **0.25**, ordering **>95%**); reads reconciled exactly against SQL (678/2/551); off-switch live; write path under rollback; then **[a live GHA run](https://github.com/QAcademy-Nurses/mynclex/actions/runs/30454262945) green in 46s reproducing the local numbers exactly** — proving the secrets correct, not merely present. Four secrets became **two** (a project URL isn't a secret; `migrate-prod.yml` already carries the prod ref as a literal). ⚠ **The maths has never met real data** — best-covered question has 4 answers, needs 30 — so 10c is built, verified, and correctly doing nothing. ⚠ **The Sunday 02:00 UTC schedule targets prod and is live from now.** **⭐ THEN SAM SPECIFIED 10d AND HALVED IT TWICE.** Asked for the snapshot follow-up "in simple terms", then took it apart. (a) **Drop the source branch** — §5.5.2 branched on `difficulty_source`, but `difficulty_irt` always holds a number and what we show is its *word equivalent*, so why branch? Right: the seeds **round-trip exactly** (verified by running the code — −2/−1/0/1/2 land dead-centre of their bands, half a logit clear of every cut-off), so for an unmeasured question both rules return the same word and the branch protects nothing. It's also **more faithful to §5.5's own purpose** — the branch *permitted* the shown difficulty to disagree with the number the engine uses, the exact drift §5.5 exists to prevent. What it really defended (a word with no number) was **a live hazard that same morning, 633 items deep**, and is unreachable now the seeding trigger exists — so the simplification is safe *because of* the earlier fix. (b) **Store the number in place of the word**, verified safe since nothing else reads it (no SQL, no report, no analytics). 10d = **six functions, one substitution each, no new column, no table migration**. Two of my own cautions retired by his reading: the `cat_item_difficulty` "two copies could drift" worry doesn't apply (same INSERT, same variable — they *cannot*), and then-and-now in review needs **no** history table (snapshot = then, bank row = now). Recorded as §5.5.2 **superseded** + new §5.5.2b. ⏭ **Next session builds 10d.** ⏭ Also not built: manual trigger · history readout · **misfit warnings**. ⚠ dev's migration tracker still drifted.](sessions/2026-07.md)
- [2026-07 — **2026-07-29 (CAT pool: the regrain + Slice 10b3 — the reservation is now ENFORCED — MERGED to `main`):** began as "verify 10b2" and turned into two corrections and a build. **5 commits, 2 migrations** (`20260822120000`, `20260823120000`), dev-applied; vitest **549 → 565**; prod still `1a6717d` — and prod does **not** carry `cat_pool` at all (10b1 never released), so §20 is a no-op there until content is reserved. **⭐ THE REGRAIN — two reservation units, not three.** Sam's correction, backed hard by the code: **a trend dataset is not a selection unit anywhere** — `_nclex_eligible_unit_pool` emits only `QUESTION` and `CASE`, trend questions travel in the standalone branch on their own row, and `cat_next_item` picks them individually (one per dataset *per exam*). Reserving a dataset reserved something nothing draws; the same panel already said so about its builder-visibility flag. Column dropped from `nclex_trend_datasets` + tutor mirror; each trend question carries its own. A **case** stays a wrapper reservation but children now hold a **trigger-maintained copy** — cascade in the DB not a Server Action, because a forgotten join is how stock leaks and a cascade in one save path is one a second forgets; **detach-clear is load-bearing** (else a detached child becomes a reserved standalone nobody ticked). All three trigger behaviours proven on real rows in a rollback. Trend tile survives as a **variety measure** (distinct datasets / 60, "reserved" never "inherited"), with the doc recording honestly that **60 datasets does not derive from the 15-sitting floor** — it's an editorial goal. **THREE DEFECTS:** ① the **"never tick a child" rule had never been enforced** — the guard tested `mode === 'wrapper-child'`, retired 2026-05-01, so it passed for everything and the tick showed on case children writing a flag the page ignored (⚠ `HousekeepingMode` is unusable for wrapper-child tests; replaced with a React context); ② the difficulty marker measured a fifth of the *standalone* target while the bars counted the whole pool; ③ **⭐ every Coverage number was truncated by PostgREST's silent 1,000-row cap** — a 2,833-question pool read **"1,000 / 2,880"** — **not caused by the regrain** (standalone was already 2,393 rows, the drawer ~1,700, both truncated since 10b2 shipped) and it **survived three rounds of tsc/vitest/SQL because none go through the client's row limit**, caught the first time a human opened the page. **⭐ SLICE 10b3 — ENFORCED:** practice excludes reserved stock via **one predicate** in the single gateway helper (count/breakdown/create follow for free); CAT draws only from the pool in both RPCs, **except the mid-case branch, ungated on purpose** so a released case can't abandon a student mid-block; ⚠ **CAT stopped requiring `is_builder_visible`** — it contradicts reservation and would have emptied the pool — which also closed a pre-existing drift between the case and standalone picks; **two layers** (reserving clears `is_builder_visible` + `is_free_sample`; the audit card becomes a drift detector); mutual exclusivity now binds from the **readiness side too**. The two big CAT functions were deployed by **transforming live definitions under assertions**, then the migration file proven **byte-identical by md5** — never retyped. **Dev: practice 3,245 → 807 units, 61 → 24 cases, both leak checks zero**, reserved-and-builder-visible 2,824 → 0, CAT keeps 2,382 candidates + 36 cases. ⚠ **A live CAT sitting has NOT been played end to end** (Sam's test). ⚠ 7 unplaceable + 159 draft reserved rows are pre-existing content gaps. ⚠ dev's migration tracker is version-drifted. ⏭ Next = **10c**, the recalibration job — §20 is complete.](sessions/2026-07.md)

An iterative, Sam-driven chain of well-scoped UI decisions, each discussed before building. **(1) Three-tab builder (#2 closed):** examined the phone filter-accordion and **left it as-is** (collapsing trades away discoverability + live counts to shorten a scroll that read fine; the 5-open/4-closed split is already considered) — but split the crowded two-tab layout into **Intent & Mode · Question pool · Content filters** so each pane has one job (`bea54d5`, pure UI, tab-key `'mode'|'filters'`→`'mode'|'pool'|'content'`); the axis-sub-panes idea considered + rejected. **(2) ⭐ Exam-mode display leaks (#10 built, `7e845fb`):** a live exam no longer shows the difficulty pill (a CAT ability readout), subject chip (Sam: strip it too), `Case N of M`, the `CJMM step` label or the CJMM strip — kept the type pill, case panel, grid. One flag `hideExamScaffold = intent==='EXAM' && isLive`, so **review restores everything**; applied to ALL exam modes. DOM-verified on standalones + a Sequential case (Sam's own session `7d8fbe63`), study control shows all of it, console clean. **(3) Intent session title (`b19aa00`):** "Practice session" → **"Study session" / "Exam session"** (singular), review too. **(4) Grid rules + topbar toggle (`cb35dce`):** the grid is **fully hidden in live Sequential + CAT** (it can't navigate there — clicks were already no-ops; question reclaims the space; gated `archetype==='SEQUENTIAL' && isLive`, so review keeps it), and everywhere it *does* show it gained a **topbar show/hide toggle** next to Mark mirroring the clock's eye — **additive, nothing retired** (Sam), all controls drive one `gridOpen`. **(5) Real toggle icons (`163d6c4`):** the clock `◉/◌` glyphs → a **clock face** (plain/slashed; Sam picked clock over eye — an eye doesn't name the subject), and the grid toggle got the **matching plain/slashed** treatment. **(6) Calculator scheduled as slice #16 (`b1a7434`):** discussed placeholder-vs-build; a disabled calc withholds a tool the student *needs*, so Sam scheduled it as a proper slice — basic 4-function, draggable pop-up, every runner mode, and (Sam) built once as an **app-wide reusable widget**, not runner-only. **Dev cleanup:** deleted the 7 test attempts created this session (safe; 0 orphans). ⚠ runner mobile still unoptimised (separate future pass). ⏭ Open: #11 `/help/cat` · #12 Slice 10 recalibration · #13 reopened CAT decisions · #16 calculator. ⚠ `PAYSTACK_SECRET_KEY` still not on prod.](sessions/2026-07.md)
- [2026-07 — **2026-07-25:** **THE ENGAGEMENT CLOCK built + merged, plus the small fixes, the mode-name decision, and the resume rule** (branch `claude/work-session-4fe7ee`; **9 commits — first 6 merged to `main` `6287478→76fe846`, last 3 on the branch**; app-layer + **one migration** `20260815120000` dev-applied; tsc clean bar the 2 known `scoring-roundtrip` errors; eslint adds nothing new; NOT prod). **(1) Small fixes #3/#4/#5:** the public demo's CAT button → "Go to CAT" (`f0b5901`); five stale slice-number build notes on the tutor library retired, all describing shipped features, + the dormant `comingIn` template de-jargoned (`0266425`); the **💡 bulb closed as NOT broken** — opened a live curator bulb, it renders as a clean spaced list; the note had over-generalised the dashboard's already-fixed squashing. **(2) Mode names #1:** Sam kept the interim labels; §15.1 parked as a future alternative, with **"Tutor" flagged** as colliding with MyNclex's real tutor role (a collision UWorld — the source term — doesn't have) (`6f98526`). **(3) ⭐ THE ENGAGEMENT CLOCK #6** (`da850cc`) — the main build. `(STUDY, TIMED_FREE_NAV)` now counts ENGAGED time: the countdown **freezes while the page is hidden and resumes on return, durably across a full close days later** — `remaining = duration − engaged_seconds_used`, persisted server-side. **Whole design settled with Sam first:** only Study→Timed practice (EXAM stays wall-clock on purpose, same mode id under EXAM unchanged); "away" = page hidden incl. tab-switch (reusing the per-question engine's signal); away-time forgiven **in full**; **no heartbeat** — saves on submit/nav/page-hide, a crash mid-question forgives that question's time in the student's favour. Migration `20260815120000` (column + **mode-guarded** monotonic RPC that can't touch an exam clock); **the load-bearing fix** = `page.tsx` lazy expiry tests engaged≥duration not wall, else a reopened attempt is force-killed. **Verified live:** froze 8s hidden; DB engaged=42 matched screen while wall=242; **reload resumed at 900−42 not 900−242**; EXAM control (same mode id) drained while hidden + column stayed NULL. **Also closed #7** (Study-timed ≡ Exam-timed) **and #9** (intent drove no behaviour). **Sam tested on a real device + approved; merged to `main`.** ⚠ Known minor: an engagement *timeout* still back-dates `ended_at` (rare, cosmetic, needs an RPC — RLS blocks a direct fix; deferred). **(4) Resume rule #8** (`1a64969`, not yet merged) — Sam's insight: the behaviour was never broken, the **copy** was. An Exam **is** resumable while its wall clock runs; the Builder falsely said "cannot be resumed". Settled to the code's coherent behaviour (**every non-CAT attempt resumable; clock waits for Study, keeps running for Exam**); fixed the Exam start-note on the real builder + demo (**CAT split out**, keeps "cannot be resumed"), corrected the stale fetcher comment, reconciled both plan docs. No behaviour change, no migration. ⏭ Open: #2 filter accordion · #10 §16.6 leaks · #11 `/help/cat` · #12 Slice 10 · #13 the 2 reopened CAT decisions. ⚠ `PAYSTACK_SECRET_KEY` still not on prod.](sessions/2026-07.md)
- [2026-07 — **2026-07-24 (later):** **THE MODES — investigated, two cut, renamed, and the stale copy behind them — MERGED to `main`** (same branch `claude/work-session-fad16e`; **6 commits**; app-layer + **one migration** `20260814120000` dev-applied; tsc clean bar the 2 known `scoring-roundtrip` errors; eslint clean on new files, the 13 in `runner.tsx` pre-existing and confirmed by stashing; NOT prod). **Began as an investigation, not a build.** Reading the mode plan against the code found that the framework lives in **three documents** and the one meant to unify them — runner **§4 "Per-mode behaviour matrix"**, which lists exactly the axes Sam asked about — reads **"To be filled in."** That empty section is the root of everything below. **FOUR FINDINGS:** ① **the engagement clock does not exist** — the builder promises STUDY students a clock that "pauses if you step away", but runner §8 (settled *later*, 2026-05-09) derives the countdown from `started_at + duration_seconds` with **no intent branch**, the code matches §8, and there are **no pause columns**; the word "engagement" is real but belongs to the per-question time engine, which §8 says explicitly does not sum with this clock. ② **STUDY-timed and EXAM-timed are mechanically identical** (same 90s×count duration from a mode-only trigger, same wall clock, nav, feedback, submit) — a consequence of ①. ③ **resume is a THREE-WAY contradiction** — parent §15 "impossible for any timed mode", §6.1.2 "STUDY intent only", and the code (slice 4.5a) resumes **everything except CAT** with no intent condition, while the Builder tells EXAM students "cannot be resumed" and an abandoned EXAM attempt **does** reappear in the Resume banner. ④ **`intent` drives NO behaviour** — read in exactly one place across the whole session route, to print the word "Study" or "Exam". **THE TWO CUTS.** Sam's instinct that some modes conflict was supported more sharply than "conflicting": **each removed tuple was mechanically identical to its twin under the other intent**, so each was a duplicate card distinguished only by a label the runner does not honour. `(EXAM, Untimed Test)` — untimed = `duration_seconds NULL`, so no clock either way, and "exam framing without time pressure" is what STUDY already is. `(STUDY, Timed Sequential)` — forward-only reproduces exam mechanics (NCSBN: answer in order, no going back, no skipping) and teaches nothing; an exam constraint wearing a study label. **⚠ The blast radius reached the tutor system:** `nclex_tutor_quizzes` has **no intent column** (intent is DERIVED at launch, MOCK→EXAM / PRACTICE→STUDY), so its CHECK is a mirror and would have let a tutor author a quiz that **exploded on launch**; MOCK lost Untimed Test, PRACTICE lost Timed Sequential, **7 pairs → 5**, and the migration **refuses to run** if a quiz uses one rather than silently rewriting a tutor's mode. **Three dev attempt rows CONVERTED not deleted** (one is COMPLETED — deleting it punches a hole in a student's history for a naming decision they never made), which is also **load-bearing for display**: four read paths resolve labels from these arrays and fall back to the raw id. **Verified against the DB, not assumed** — conversions landed exactly as predicted, and **both removed tuples were proven actively REJECTED by a live rollback-only UPDATE**, not read off the constraint definition. **THE NAMING ARC.** The structural unlock: *"Untimed Learning" and "Untimed Test" are named after the axis they SHARE, not the one that separates them* — the real difference is **when the truth arrives**. A **web search** grounded the options (AMBOSS ships "Study Mode"/"Exam Mode" as its two frames, validating our *intent* names and ruling those words out one level down; **"Tutor" is the NCLEX-native term** across UWorld/Archer/Bootcamp; UWorld's "Untimed" == our Untimed Test; NCSBN's own rules make forward-only the *authentic* mode). Recorded as **§15.1 PROPOSED-NOT-APPLIED** with the reasoning for **rejecting numbered mode names** and a naming avoid-list. Sam then set **interim** labels — STUDY: Learning · Untimed practice · Timed practice; EXAM: Free Navigation · Sequential · Computer Adaptive Testing (CAT) — dropping "Exams" after it read twice on surfaces that already prefix with the intent. **⚠ The rename exposed FOUR competing label lists** — `filter-config`'s two arrays plus hardcoded mode-keyed maps in `runner.tsx`, `preflight.tsx` and `lib/tutor-quiz/format.ts`, already drifted into **three spellings of one mode**. All four now resolve through one `modeLabelFor(intent, mode)`; `formatQuizMode` **requires** the quiz kind, **proven on real rows** (the same mode id renders "Timed practice" for the PRACTICE quiz and "Free Navigation" for the MOCK). A tutor had been configuring quizzes in words no student ever reads. Public demo resynced, **stays hardcoded** by Sam's call. **The intent card**: icon and name now share a row, **159px → 128px**, landing twice on mobile where the cards stack. **⚠ STALE COPY — Sam spotted two, looking found three more:** the **preflight showed a build note to students** ("In 4.1 every mode runs with per-question submit ... land with slice 4.5", false since 4.5 shipped); the **footer described the wrong mode** — Untimed-Learning copy for EVERY non-CAT mode, so a student in a timed sequential exam with Prev disabled was told to expect a rationale after each submit; **"Next → →"**, a **double arrow on the most common button in the runner** on every question but the last, in every mode (three labels baked an arrow in, the footer appends one); the Mark tooltip read "Mark-for-review · slice 4.7"; and the footer's fallback hint was "MCQ wiring lands in slice 4.1.4". New `lib/practice/runner/mode-brief.ts` holds the copy **and** the archetype mapping so the two cannot drift, and **deliberately omits the "pauses if you step away" claim** from the preflight — the last screen before a student commits to a sitting should not promise a clock we haven't built. **All 15 open items captured at the top of BUILD_LIST as "⏭ THE OPEN LIST"**, grouped by what each needs, with **Sam's pick for next session recorded: the engagement clock** plus its two design questions. ⚠ prod still at `1a6717d`; next release ships migration `20260814120000` (a no-op on prod data). ⚠ `PAYSTACK_SECRET_KEY` still not on prod.](sessions/2026-07.md)

Measured in-browser: page `scrollWidth` stayed at exactly 375, so **the clipped 31px was UNREACHABLE, not merely off-screen** — the `+` stepper and recap values were gone, not cut. **⚠ THE FINDING CD COULD NOT HAVE KNOWN:** `/student/bank/practice` is **one of the four student mobile tabs**, so at ≤768px the page already carries a fixed 56px bottom tab bar — **CD's sticky action bar lands exactly on it**. Same class of gap as the dashboard's six data mismatches. **The approved shape:** on a phone there is no "beside", so the workbench becomes a form filled top-to-bottom — and because `<SummaryPanel>` is already the **last child**, stacking lands it at the end of the form **for free, so NOTHING had to be dropped** (CD shed the recap, breakdown, stepper and start note only *because* it chose a bar). What the phone still needs while scrolling is re-supplied by a **61px fixed launch strip** (count + Start only), **z-index 19** so it stays under the drawer/sheet; it earns its space because the Filters tab is **~3,000px** tall, so without it a filter change means scrolling three screens to launch. **One shared `startLabelFor()`** stops the strip and panel drifting; the strip takes the short form but **keeps Quiz/Exam** (the difference between a session you can pause and one you cannot). **CAT reads `MIN_ITEMS`/`MAX_ITEMS`** — the handoff still carries the pre-NGN **"75–145"** §9 corrected to 85–150 *and* still starts a CAT from the builder, which §10.7.1 forbids; both would have shipped if the prototype were followed literally. **⚠ THREE DEFECTS FOUND BY LOOKING AT THE SCREEN**, none visible in the CSS: the **resume banner rendered ONE WORD PER LINE** (icon and button pair both `flex-shrink:0`, so the text absorbed the whole squeeze to ~50px); the **tab strip broke into four ragged fragments**; and the header said **"the count on the right"**, which on a phone pointed at nothing. **Verified live:** work column **0 → 315px**, **zero horizontal overflow at 375 AND 320px**, strip flush on the tab bar, count tracked a real filter change (**2,024 → 2,361**) and **agreed with the panel exactly**, CAT read **"85–150 / Go to CAT"** on both surfaces, console clean, **desktop confirmed untouched**. **Not run: vitest** — no test imports the touched files and this worktree has no local `node_modules`, the exact condition that leaves a stray dir breaking Turbopack. ⏭ **NOT merged to `main`** (awaiting Sam's phone test) · docs held for go-ahead (`mobile-responsive.md`; marking the CAT doorway built) · the **accordion question** deferred to a real-phone judgement (5 of 9 axes default open). ⚠ `PAYSTACK_SECRET_KEY` still not on prod.](sessions/2026-07.md)
- [2026-07 — **2026-07-23:** **CAT SLICE 9 — the student Bank Dashboard, built in four stages and MERGED to `main`** (branch `claude/work-session-ad4124`; **7 commits**; app-layer, **no migration**; tsc clean bar the 2 known `scoring-roundtrip` errors; eslint clean; vitest **393 → 412**, 341 at session start; NOT prod). **The concept changed before any code:** the previous session left cards 8 (readiness ring) and 9 (doorways) unresolved because both needed data we didn't have — so Sam reshaped them around data we can read, took it back to Claude Design, and returned with a new canvas; **variant 2d** chosen (readiness as its own panel, each signal carrying the real signature graph from its own page). **Six things the prototype said that our data doesn't**, caught before building by checking the live schema instead of the design's story: the **readiness pack has no pass/fail** (it returns one of four band words — "Passed" appears nowhere in the product, and its 65% pass tick isn't our number, 75 is); there is **no "At standard"** for a CAT (the verdict is binary, so the 3-step meter invented a rung that can never light); a **red 55% pill** on a practice quiz invents a pass mark practice sets don't have; the **1,500 target was invented**; the **CAT mini-chart didn't read at 120px**; and — **the finding that mattered most** — **the design could punish a student for what she hadn't bought**: two of four signals are separate purchases and ○ meant both "you fell short" and "you never attempted this", so a bank-only student with 2,000 answers at 78% would read *"Not yet ready — 1 of 4"* with no route to the top band that didn't involve spending more. **Four decisions replaced the readiness card**, built into one pure file (27 tests) so none can erode into the view: **three signal states** (met / not met / **not yet attempted**, band counted over what was ATTEMPTED, unattempted signals rendering as doorways); **the band reuses the packs' own words** (Building / Approaching / Ready — without it the page carried *three* vocabularies for one idea; **Excelling excluded** since a pack earns that with a real score, and **Ready needs ≥2 attempted signals**); **volume is the GATE, not a signal** (results say how well you did, volume says how much we know — as a fourth signal 1,600 answers at 45% would tick a box and read as progress; below the gate the band is withheld, **which also supplies the first-week empty state the handoff left undesigned**); and **the copy never promises odds** — Sam's framing was "we could do an estimate of your chances", but that needs outcome data from nurses who've sat the NCLEX, so the threshold buys **reliability** and a test asserts "chance"/"probability" never appear. **A level correction the plan had wrong:** §19.4 called them "the 8 **L1 category** bars" — there are only **four** L1 categories; the eight are **L2 subcategories**, which is also the level `weakestSubcategory()` and the builder's `?subcat=` deep link already speak, so **"Drill it" reuses the readiness report's existing link**. **The streak was shared, then deliberately un-shared** on Sam's call (two homes measuring different achievements should be free to define a "study day" differently — **which they immediately do**): the bank's now requires an **ANSWERED question**, not an attempt row, because a streak you can hold by opening a quiz and closing the tab isn't measuring study; verified in SQL *before* looking — the strict rule removed exactly the two days an attempt was opened and nothing answered (best run 6 → 5). **⚠ Two cap bugs, one already live:** the History doorway reported `getHistoryAttempts()`'s **50-row limit** as a real total (true figure 51) and would have said 50 for ever. **Found by looking:** the shared 💡 bulb opened **off-screen** (it anchors `right:0` for curator toolbars where it *ends* a row) then overflowed the other way at 375px; **its body has no stylesheet anywhere in the repo**, so every bulb's `<ul>` loses its markers to the global reset (**⬜ still true of the curator bulbs**); Sam caught the 💡 used as **decoration** on the lowest-scoring card (it's the help affordance — a bulb that does nothing costs us the real ones → now a flag) and the **missing doorway icons**; and a bar reading **"100%" off 10 questions** sat above an 88-question bar at 59%, the least-tested area looking like the strongest (counts now shown). **Verified against SQL not eyeballs** (accuracy 55% = 159/287, all 8 bars, 51 sessions, 3 CATs), and **two panel states no account could show were forced and reverted** (the band; the invitations). **⚠ NOT tested by Sam** — he was tired and instructed the merge without a test or audit, so the first user-eyes pass is outstanding, as is review of the 75% bar and the ≥2-signals rule. ⏭ open: Slice 9's other two pieces (Builder EXAM-intent CAT option · public `/help/cat`) · §16.6 display leaks. ⚠ `PAYSTACK_SECRET_KEY` still not on prod.](sessions/2026-07.md)

- [2026-07 — **2026-07-22:** **CAT — the timeout defect FIXED, and the second defect it was hiding** (branch `claude/work-session-ba4acf`; 2 build commits + docs, merged to `main`; app-layer + **one additive migration** `20260813120000` dev-applied; tsc clean bar the 2 known `scoring-roundtrip` errors; eslint clean; vitest **306 → 328**). **(1) A timed-out CAT now gets a verdict** (`2088352`). `checkTermination`'s `TIME_LIMIT_HIT` branch was correct and tested but reachable only from `playTurn`, which needs an answer to score — and a time-out is exactly the case with **no answer**; meanwhile the runner's mode-blind auto-expire fired at zero and wrote none of the `cat_*` columns, winning every race, so the branch was **dead code**. Fix = `lib/practice/cat/expire.ts`, a sibling of `playTurn` **minus the scoring step**, reusing **the same `checkTermination`** so a time-out *between* turns cannot resolve differently from one *inside* a turn (the property that made a separate SQL implementation the wrong shape). Branch sits in `expireAttemptAction` because **both** triggers call it; elapsed derived **server-side** (this decides a verdict with no answer behind it). Two deliberate differences from `nclex_expire_attempt`: **no `final_score`** (§13.5 — a number no surface may display is one that eventually leaks onto one) and **`ended_at` back-dated** to the true deadline; *planned as "back-date both", corrected while building* — the engine's `NOW()` is right because an answer just landed there. **The duplicated 4-hour constant collapsed** onto the attempt row (required + undefaulted, plus a test that reads the migration file), which is **what makes §9.3 a genuine one-constant change** — a move to 5 h applies to new exams while an in-progress exam keeps its own limit. **A guard the tests corrected:** asserting the rule must name `TIME_LIMIT_HIT` was wrong (confidence is checked first by design), so the precondition is now *the clock*, checked directly. **Verified against the real path**, not a simulation — a cloned live CAT back-dated past its limit, actual lazy expiry fired in-browser: `TIMED_OUT` · `TIME_LIMIT_HIT` · `BELOW_STANDARD` · **48** items · `ended_at` back-dated exactly · `final_score` NULL · one `SKIPPED` row. **48 not 49** is the detail — 49 served, the 49th on screen unanswered; counting it would inflate "You answered N questions" *and* could tip a student over the 85-item line on a question they never saw. **(2) ⚠ THE SECOND DEFECT — the unmeasured ending** (`108bf97`). With a timeout finally reaching the report it read **"Below standard"** directly above **"~93% confident you're above our standard"** — on real data. **Not an implementation slip:** `report-derive` quoted **§13.1 verbatim**. §13.1's two variants both read the verdict as though it *follows* the estimate (true for `CONFIDENCE_REACHED`, `MAX_ITEMS_HIT`, and a time-out with 85+), but a **sub-85 time-out** is forced BELOW on insufficient evidence whatever the estimate says — the verdict stops following the estimate while the probability carries on being computed from it. §13.1 *half*-anticipated it via the near-boundary variant, but that sorts by how **confident** we are (40–60%) and this case is about how much **evidence** we have; they come apart exactly here (run out of time at Q48 while sitting at 93%). **Latent since §13.1 was written, invisible only because the first defect stopped anything reaching the page — one bug was hiding the other.** Settled with Sam + built same session: the **result is a fail** and the **measurement is declined**, stated separately; the fail **leads in the headline** (*"Not passed — you ran out of time"*) because *"not enough information"* as a headline reads as a free do-over; **no probability anywhere on the variant** (ring shows the evidence gap *"48 / of 85 needed"*; a test asserts no `%` / no "confident"); the body advises on **pace, not content** — every other variant sends the student to the category breakdown, which here would send them back to do the same thing again — with all figures derived so §9.3 moving to 5 h needs no copy edit. **Display-only: `cat_verdict` stays `BELOW_STANDARD`**, no overrides — but one shared `isUnmeasured()` keeps three surfaces in step (report · history row *"Not passed — ran out of time"* · compare strip **"Timed out"** not "Below", dot still plots since the estimate is real). Category breakdown kept (Sam's call); the **85-item cliff** known and accepted. **Two things fixed by looking:** the pace read *"closer to 1.6 minutes"* like a spreadsheet (now **95 seconds**, still derived) and the strip's 52px column wrapped "Timed out" onto two lines. Verified desktop + 375px, console clean. **(3) SLICE 6c — the transition escalation, on a now-real idempotency guard.** The last piece of Slice 6, two parts (cat.html §19.4.8). **Part 1 — the idempotency guard was inert.** §19.4.4's `p_expected_item_id` was meant to catch the lost-response retry, but `turn.ts` set it from its OWN `loadCurrent` (= server's newest), so it always equalled newest and the replay branch could never fire — built, tested at the wrong altitude, unreachable, **same shape as the §19.4.7 dead code**. Never bit because a lost-response retry was a rare manual act; **6c's Retry button makes it routine**, so it was fixed first: `expectedItemId` now comes from the CLIENT, and `turn.ts` **honours a replay** (the stale score + decision are discarded — else a replay whose stale decision was `stop` would falsely complete a still-live attempt); the real replay test §19.4.4 lacked now exists. **Part 2 — the escalation.** dim (0–300ms, no spinner) → spinner (300ms) → "Still loading…" (3s) → Retry (10s), plus an error phase (message + Retry) on failure. Pure state machine (`turn-transition.ts`, 16 tests) + thin timer hook + overlay (`cat-transition.tsx`, OUTSIDE the inert wrapper, contained panel, `role="status"`/`aria-live`); footer dead while blocking so the overlay's Retry is the single way on. Verified live desktop (latency/failure injected, reverted); mobile taken structurally (≤300px centred card can't overflow). vitest 328 → **341**, no migration. **⚠ Seen on screen, not 6c:** a live CAT shows a "Difficulty · Medium" chip — the §16.6 leak, now NEXT. ⚠ Recurring session-start gotcha: vitest before `next dev` in a fresh worktree leaves a stray `node_modules/.vite` that breaks Turbopack root resolution (same as 2026-07-13) — remove `node_modules/`. ⏭ NEXT = §16.6 exam-mode display leaks · Slices 8–9; open: whether pre-fix dev attempt `4113c191` is backfilled or left as an artifact; dev fixture `aaaa1111-…0001` is a cleanup candidate. ⚠ `PAYSTACK_SECRET_KEY` still not on prod.](sessions/2026-07.md)

Began as Slice 7's loose end — the report existed but nothing at termination pointed at it — and two rounds of Sam's pushback turned it into three defects, one on the engine. **(1) The popup** (`09975c0`): a finished CAT fell into the bank-Builder variant — *"Session complete"*, a raw **"X of N correct"**, *Build another* — i.e. the exact number §13.5 forbids, and the first real CAT read **41.6% under a 98%-confident pass**. New near-empty CAT variant: the exam ended, why, one route onward. **Branch on `mode` not `source`** (a CAT is stored `CUSTOM_BUILT`); **`isCat` is a prop not context** (via context there is one frame where it would render the forbidden score); the reason sentence **reuses the report's `itemsAdministeredLine()`** so the two cannot disagree. Sam's **`source='CAT'` proposal agreed in principle but deliberately deferred** — three gates (incl. the bank subscription check) apply to CATs *only* because they look CUSTOM_BUILT, plus two coupled CHECKs. **(2) "Are you sure bug 1 was fixed?"** — it wasn't (`296e789`): the exit was fixed in the popup **only**, while `resolveAttemptExitHref` feeds four *other* exits; the resolver is now CAT-aware with `mode` **required** so no call site can silently inherit the Builder destination. Checking it exposed a real bug — the popup read **"You answered 0 questions. The engine reached confidence at question 0."** with 49 in the grid: `itemsAdministeredLine()` was reused **outside its precondition** (safe on the report, which only calls it once a verdict exists). Verdict/no-verdict now explicit and travelling as one object. **(3) ⚠ THE FINDING — a CAT that times out is NEVER reported.** Sam asked whether a student at 70 who times out gets reported: **no — and it is the normal path.** `checkTermination` case 3 resolves the clock exactly as §9.3/§9.4 specify, **with a verdict**, but only runs **inside a turn**, and the runner's **mode-blind auto-expire** fires the instant the countdown hits zero, writing none of the CAT columns. **The auto-expire always wins, so `TIME_LIMIT_HIT` is effectively dead code** — a student who answers 149 and times out on the last is told the exam "ended early" while the estimate sits in `cat_theta_before`. Confirmed on dev `4113c191` (TIMED_OUT, 49 items, `ended_at` = `started_at + 4:00:00` exactly, all 3 CAT cols NULL). **A correction kept in the record:** first written up as *"a CAT shows no clock"* off an "Untimed" pill that is the **review-mode** label on a finished attempt — Sam pushed back, live CATs **do** show a countdown, and correcting it made the defect **worse** (with a clock running visibly to zero, the silent auto-expire is simply what a timeout *is*). Written up as cat.html **§19.4.6** + placed at the **top of the carried-forward list as a defect, not a decision**, sequenced with the reopened §9.3. **(4) The Builder's CAT card** (`d8b11c3`): **"75–145" in three places** was the **pre-NGN pair** left behind when §9 corrected to 85–150 — now interpolated from `MIN_ITEMS`/`MAX_ITEMS`; and **"Start Exam — CAT" actually started something that was not a CAT** (the **Builder's** RPC with `mode='CAT'` + a hardcoded 75 → an ordinary attempt with no engine state). **§10.7.1 settled the doorway on 2026-07-19 and it was never built** — its instruction was "do not wire it to `create_cat_attempt`"; it had been wired to the *wrong* RPC instead. The surface a student lands on when a CAT ends and re-opens from history — all seven §13 regions in priority order (verdict → readiness probability → items administered → trajectory → Client Needs breakdown → clinical judgment → compare-to-previous → CTAs), with the **abandoned CAT as its own page** rather than the report blanked. New pure `report-derive.ts` (+26 tests) · loader `report.ts` · route `student/bank/cat/result/[attemptId]/` · `.catr-*` mobile-first CSS; CAT-home history rows now link in. **Probability + clinical judgment are derived on read, never stored** — the probability via the same `readinessProbability()` the stopping rule calls at a different threshold, so page and engine cannot disagree. **The trajectory shift** (engine stores theta *before* each question, graph plots *after*, so each point takes the NEXT item's before-value) is guarded by three tests — getting it wrong draws a plausible graph lagging by one question and nothing else would catch it; the shaded band is the real 95% interval from the engine's own `CONFIDENCE_Z`, which is what makes the §9.5 ceiling case legible. **Two CD rounds:** Sam cut the raw **θ / standard-error tiles** ("a student can't even understand what that is") + duration (a v2 leak in my brief) + the **per-result calibration note** (§3 settles it as a one-time help-page disclosure), kept the chip grid; round 2 also added a **"take another CAT"** CTA — a gap in §13 itself, whose Below-standard copy promises a next sitting and offered no route. **⚠ THE FINDING (on screen, from real data):** categories read **19–48% beside "Above standard — 98% confident"** — **not a bug**: mean item difficulty 0.765 vs ability 0.48, so Rasch predicts ~43% and the student scored **41.6%**. A CAT serves at the edge of ability, so **raw success converges toward half for everyone at every level** — absolute category percentages are near-uninformative, and §13.2's footnote only ever anticipated the smaller *partial-credit* effect. Footnote now names both + "read them against each other, not out of 100"; rows weakest-first. **This makes §13.6's drill-down + remediation the strongest remaining gap.** **Two decisions REOPENED, noted not changed:** **§9.3** the 4-hour limit (real NCLEX gives 5; contradicts §9.2's "match NCLEX exactly" principle, a ceiling can only cut off slow students so "less punishing" is inverted, and 1.4 min/item puts 150 items at ~3h35m) and **§9.1.1 the passing standard** — **it was never independently chosen**: `PRIOR_MEAN`, Medium, and `PASSING_THETA` are all zero for unrelated reasons, so "Above standard" means "better than the middle of our own difficulty labelling"; Sam: for an exam this consequential passing shouldn't sit at the midpoint by construction; the real run cleared 0.0 by **0.025**, so a bar 0.1 higher changes exam length and verdicts; ⚠ a 0.5 bar would dissolve §7.7's saturation — a consequence NOT a reason; root cause is the **three-rung ladder** so widen the spread first; route out is **standard setting** (NCSBN uses expert panels, not pass statistics); ⚠ changing it **re-interprets every CAT already sat**. **Verified live** on attempt `5b97141d` desktop + 375px: **18 case chips = 3 cases × 6 children** (§7.4 confirmed on screen), marker works, no overflow, console clean; two layout bugs found by looking (desktop grid stranded the items line in a verdict-height row → explicit rail/main wrappers; chart label collided with the trace with no safe side → padding reserved outside the plot).
